  - **What the hook guarantees:** focus moves into the dialog on open (autoFocus child, else first focusable, else the panel — hence `tabIndex={-1}` on every panel); Tab / Shift+Tab wrap at both ends and pull focus back if it escaped; Escape calls `onClose`; everything outside the dialog is `inert` + `aria-hidden` while open; focus returns to the trigger, or to the nearest surviving ancestor, or to `<body>` — never to nothing.
  - **Two orderings that are load-bearing and were got wrong first.** Focus moves in **before** the background is marked — `aria-hidden` on a subtree that still holds focus is what makes a browser dump focus to `<body>`; and the background is un-marked **before** focus is restored, because focusing a still-`inert` ancestor is a silent no-op. Both are pinned by contract tests, not just comments.
  - **The trigger is captured during render, not in an effect.** On the render where the dialog activates, the panel is not committed yet and React's `autoFocus` has not run, so `document.activeElement` is still the real trigger. Capturing it in an effect records the autoFocus'd input *inside* the panel — an element that unmounts on close, which is exactly how the focus return was lost before. Reading `document.activeElement` during render is deliberate here and costs no lint suppression; the value is handed to the trap effect through a ref written by a dependency-free effect declared ahead of it, so React's sibling effect ordering guarantees the trap sees the pre-open value.
  - **Nested dialogs answer for themselves.** A module-level stack means only the top-most dialog reacts to Tab/Escape, and each dialog restores focus to *its own* trigger, so `PhotoCropEditor` opened from the Profile sheet / Settings member Modal closes one layer at a time. Previously the cropper had no Escape handler at all, which "worked" only because nothing else was listening; adding one naively would have closed both dialogs on a single keypress.
  - **Escape and unsaved input: one documented switch, not a heuristic.** `escapeDisabled` is set only where a destructive confirmation is genuinely mid-flight and dismissing it would strand a half-applied state: `AlarmPinModal` (alarm arm/disarm in flight), `WallPinPad` and the `TransactionLogger` / `CreateCapsuleForm` / `ContentUploader` writes. Editors with unsaved draft input (the recipe sheets) keep Escape-closes as audit 5.4 specified — that is a deliberate, recorded scope decision, not an oversight.
  - **Duplicate listeners removed, not layered.** `RecipeModal`, `RecipeSearchModal`, `RecipeImportModal`, `CookMode`, `PhotoCropEditor` and `WeatherWidget` each carried their own bespoke `keydown` listener; those are gone, so no dialog closes twice per Escape. `WeatherWidget` also had a real bug here: its Escape bypassed `closeDetails`, so it closed **without** returning focus; Escape now goes through the same close path as the button. Its timeline scrubber's own Escape (reset the previewed hour) is untouched and is the one written exception in the contract test. Per-field Enter/arrow handlers are unrelated and stay legal.
  - **Unchanged on purpose:** `prefers-reduced-motion` still governs only how fast a dialog *leaves* — focus management never reads it, pinned by a contract test. The wall-mode 64px control floor is intact: `WallPinPad`'s keypad keeps its `h-[72px]` keys and `Cancel`'s `h-16`, and CookMode keeps its `min-h-[44px]` targets. `EmergencyButton`'s copy and cooldown logic are untouched; it renders the shared `Modal` and inherits the contract.
  - **Tests:** 53 new (`dialog-a11y-hook` 19 — focus-in, autoFocus precedence, panel-focus fallback, both Tab wraps, escape-pull, the disabled-fieldset rule, `escapeDisabled`, inert/aria-hidden, trigger return, the autoFocus-unmount regression, ancestor and `<body>` fallbacks, nested Escape, nested two-level restore, re-capture on reopen; `dialog-a11y-pin-dialogs` 15 across `PinModal`/`WallPinPad`/`AlarmPinModal`; `dialog-a11y-fullscreen-editors` 12 across `CookMode`/`RecipeModal`; `dialog-a11y-contract` 7 source scans that make a NEW unwired `aria-modal` dialog a CI failure). Every assertion is on `document.activeElement`, since jsdom provides real focus behaviour. **Verification:** the focused matrix of 57 files / 701 tests passed, including the existing Modal semantics (10), modal-adoption, modal-portal-containment, all 10 emergency suites, all 9 wall suites, all 13 kid suites, cook-mode, photo-crop/avatar crop, the tasks PIN flows, the settings sign-in/emergency dialogs and the tap-target, warm-glass-contract and compact-type-floor guards. Typecheck clean for every file touched; targeted ESLint **0 errors** with 7 pre-existing warnings on lines this change does not touch (`<img>` usage, `alt`, MemberModal's `resetForm` exhaustive-deps).
  - **Ops:** ordinary frontend redeploy. No API route, no PB schema change → no `npm run pb:seed`, no migration, no dependency change, no new env var and no new secret requirement.
- 2026-10-02 — fix(weather): Monster hero digits simplified + shallow 3D sections — **Files:** `src/components/ui/WxToys.tsx`, `src/components/ui/WeatherWidget.tsx`, `tests/unit/monster-skin.test.ts`, `tests/unit/weather-widget.test.tsx`, `docs/DESIGN.md`. **Visual:** Replaced the overbuilt capsule-creature temp glyphs with recognizable seven-segment numerals built from flat, differently colored rounded rectangles. Each section gets a darkened SVG copy offset behind it for shallow 3D depth; each numeric digit gets one simple white eye/dark pupil; minus has no eye. Removed knots, glints, sleeping-face arcs and mouth ornaments from hero digits. The simple number structure is closer to the user's Monsters reference and remains readable as temperature data. The condition icons and clouds are unchanged in this pass (the earlier cloudy `cloud-bars` decoration remains removed). **Tests:** `monster-skin.test.ts` pins each glyph's segment map, flat fills, depth faces and eye; focused Monster/weather 211/211; full suite 4972/4972; typecheck clean; targeted ESLint clean. Local Playwright review at 390px + desktop showed the live temperature and section extrusion without layout overflow. Impeccable updated locally to v4.5.0 per user request.
- 2026-10-02 — fix(chat,tools): a fix-wave review of the chat-reliability wave, which itself introduced two regressions — **Files:** `src/lib/chat-store.ts`, `src/app/chat/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/hermes/chat/route.ts`, `src/lib/consuela/live-reads.ts`, `src/lib/hermes-tools.ts`, `tests/unit/chat-thinking-ui.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/chat-store.test.ts`, `tests/unit/hermes-tools-live-reads.test.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.7, `AGENTS.md`, this changelog. Four commits: `8266ce1` / `3d87a49` / `aa91bb9` / `6da3d1f`.
  - **(1) Important, a regression from `7c1193e` — turn 2 lost the live chips AND the live transcript for the whole tool-gathering phase.** The page gated the live surfaces on "the newest assistant row in the whole visible thread owns activity", but a **finished** turn keeps its `thinking` / `toolEvents` for the life of the page — `stripVolatile` guards *persistence*, not the live tree — so from turn 2 onward that row always owned the activity and the gate was true from the moment `send()` set `streaming: true` until turn 2's first token opened its bubble. Exactly the phase the tool rounds run in, and exactly the symptom the previous wave had cured one level up. **Fix:** `chat-store` publishes `liveTurnId` synchronously in `send()` (the row does not exist before the first token, so it has to be published with the turn, not at finalize) and clears it in the `finally`, and the page gates on *that* anchor. The handoff stays atomic — the finalize write is still what makes the live row own the activity, so the two copies never coexist across the reconcile await. **Why it got through:** every case in `chat-thinking-ui.test.tsx` renders a fresh page and drives exactly one turn. The new case sends **two** turns on one mount and asserts turn 2's running chip and live transcript are on screen while it gathers; RED before the fix (`expected <span data-state="ok"> to have a length of 2 but got 1`).
  - **(2) Important — a stop pressed after `[DONE]` silently deleted the answer from the family thread.** `73721c1` moved the terminator before `persistChatPair` (correctly — it closed the resurrection) but left the store nulling `abortController` in its `finally`, which runs after the finalize writes **and** after `await fetchPBThread(...)`. `UnifiedInput` shows an always-enabled stop button for as long as `streaming` is true, so for that whole window a stop aborted a stream that had already produced everything: the composed fetch signal tore the socket down, the route saw `request.signal.aborted`, and `persistChatPair` was skipped. The user watched a complete answer that reached no other device, with no "Stopped." and no error. Pre-`73721c1` the persist had already finished before `[DONE]`, so this could not happen — that commit opened the window and only narrowed it. **Fix:** `send()` sets `abortController = null` immediately after `streamConsuelaChat` resolves — before the finalize writes and before the reconcile await — so a stop pressed once the answer is on screen cannot reach the socket. `streamInFlight` stays true until the `finally`, so the thread still takes no second turn mid-persist. **Trade-off, stated:** the stop button stays visible for the reconcile round trip and is now a no-op there, which is the correct failure (a few hundred ms of an unresponsive button) next to a deleted answer. **RED before the fix**, matching the reported repro: parked on the reconcile read and pressed stop, `streamSignal.aborted` was `true`.
  - **(3) Important, contract completion — a failed Google-calendar read still produced a confident, incomplete "today".** `mergedTodaysEvents` collapsed a failed Google leg to `[]` and returned the family rows bare, so a day whose **school** events were never read came back looking whole — school events live only in `consuela_google_calendar_events`, so this is the honest-null contract's worst case, told to a kid. Both call sites (`get_todays_events`, `get_dashboard_summary`) received only the merged array and structurally could not tell the two apart. `mergedTodaysEvents` now returns `{ rows, googleUnavailable }`; each tool appends a **`google_unavailable`** leg beside the existing `meals_error` / `tasks_error` shape — same pattern, no new vocabulary — and a dead Google sync still does **not** blank the family's own events. The honest half is held and tested in both directions: the flag is false whenever the Google leg was read successfully, however empty it came back, because a day genuinely without school events is a real answer. **Test rewritten, deliberately:** `hermes-tools-live-reads.test.ts` "still answers with the family's own events when only Google fails" pinned the conflated shape and encoded exactly the behaviour being changed. A sibling case that claimed to cover a Google read failure never did — the mock returns `[]` for an absent collection, so it has always been the genuinely-empty case — and is renamed to say what it exercises. `liveEventsRange` and the assistant-context pack are **untouched**: the pack's null-means-unreadable contract is unchanged.
  - **Minors.** (M4) The `onToolEvent` comment claimed "a repeated call in a later round keeps its own chip" — the upsert keys on the tool **name**, so a repeat replaces the earlier state (one chip, last state), and a tool that failed in round 1 and succeeded in round 3 reads as ✅. Corrected to state the collapse, its cost and why it is accepted; pinned by a new test so it is a decision, not an accident. (M5) `8ab84cf` closed the optimistic-id window *inside* `ensureHydrated`, but the composer is enabled from the first render while hydration only starts in the mount effect, so the gap before it was still open — `nextOptimisticId` now reseeds from the stored rows until hydration has begun (same monotonic, range-filtered reseed, so it cannot re-issue an id already handed out). RED without it: the send's user row landed on id 101 and replaced the stale row that already held it. (M6) The `[DONE]` comment said the terminator means "this answer is complete and will be stored"; a **Clem** turn stores nothing, so for that agent it promises delivery and nothing more. Comment-only — `ClemAssistant` was confirmed not to depend on the stronger reading.
  - **Doc corrections, because both statements were wrong.** "Known condition 4" above bounded the loss at "a stop after the last token but before `[DONE]`" and the fix-wave paragraph bounded the residual at "the frame's flight time". Between `73721c1` and this wave neither held: the window also covered the terminator's flight time, the store's reconcile round trip and the two PB inserts. Known condition 4 is restated as **"before the client has processed `[DONE]`"** (true now, and only true because of the un-stoppable settled stream) with the interim understatement named; the paragraph's "an abort racing the terminator can still leave 'Stopped.' over a stored pair" is **retracted** — an abort in that gap puts the store on its catch *and* makes the route skip the persist, so the two agree, and an abort after the gap rendered no "Stopped." either. What that gap really cost was the opposite failure, silent, and it is what (2) fixes. `[DONE]`'s meaning is corrected to "complete and handed to the requester", conditional on the persist guard, and with the Clem exception stated in both the comment and the changelog.
  - **Contracts (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §5.7, added here):** (10) a **settled** stream is un-stoppable — `chat-store` drops its abort controller when `streamConsuelaChat` resolves, so no abort can reach the socket after the client processes `[DONE]`; (11) the live chips and the live transcript are gated on the **turn in flight** (`liveTurnId`), never on the newest assistant row in the thread, because a finished turn keeps its display-only fields for the life of the page; (12) the calendar surfaces are **partial-aware**: a merged day carries `googleUnavailable`, and a partially-read day is never reported as a clear one.
  - **Ops:** ordinary image redeploy. **No PB schema change → no `npm run pb:seed`**, no migration, no dependency change, **no new env var and no new secret requirement.**
  - **Not fixed, deliberately, and recorded here rather than left silent.** `liveEventsRange` (behind `get_calendar_range` and the assistant-context calendar pack) still conflates a single-source failure the same way `mergedTodaysEvents` did. Fixing it changes `get_calendar_range`'s payload shape **and** the pack's `unavailable` vocabulary, which is a bigger surface than this review's scope and would need its own wave; `assistant-context.ts` is null-aware today and stays untouched. `ai/TOOLS.md` / `ai/KID.md` gained no row for the new `google_unavailable` leg — it rides the payload string itself, the same way the existing `events_error` / `meals_error` / `tasks_error` legs do — and adding one means regenerating `src/lib/ai-boot.generated.ts`.
- 2026-10-02 — feat(chat,tools): a reply always renders, a stopped reply never comes back, Consuela thinks out loud and can read the Hall of Fame / Skill Tree / Capsules — **Files:** `src/lib/chat-store.ts`, `src/lib/chat-stream.ts`, `src/app/api/hermes/chat/route.ts`, `src/app/chat/page.tsx`, `src/components/meals/ClemAssistant.tsx`, `src/lib/hermes-tools.ts`, `src/lib/consuela/live-reads.ts`, `src/lib/time-capsule.ts`, `src/lib/ai-boot.generated.ts`; NEW `src/components/chat/{ThinkingDisclosure,ToolActivityChips,RedeemRewardChip}.tsx`; NEW `tests/unit/{hermes-tool-writes,hermes-tool-family}.test.ts` + `tests/unit/chat-{thinking-ui,redeem-chip}.test.tsx`; MODIFIED `tests/unit/{chat-store,chat-stream,hermes-chat-stream,hermes-chat-clem,consuela-kid-soul}.test.ts`; `ai/KID.md`, `ai/TOOLS.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.7, `docs/DESIGN.md` (three UI Change Records), this changelog. 37 commits, `cd86856`…`a0802f4` + `dd57b57`/`a88f0be`/`c84e52d` (the reliability probe), then the final-review fix wave `8ab84cf`/`7c1193e`/`8fa1827`/`d20eed6` — see **Final-review fix wave** below.
  - **Reported as two symptoms.** (1) *"If the LLM replies it never shows up when it finishes — only if I refresh the page"* — the user-confirmed end state was the typing dots bouncing for a bit, then stopping, with **no response bubble**. (2) *"If I had a tool call error, I do not get a response back."* The reply was in PocketBase the whole time; refresh re-hydrated it. A refresh-only recovery is the signature of a **render-path** bug, not a transport or provider one.
  - **Root cause — an optimistic id collided with a stale localStorage row and overwrote it.** `chat-store.ts` starts `msgCounter = 100` on **every page load**, while `persistHistory()` keeps prior sessions' rows (ids 101, 102, …) and `ensureHydrated()` merges them back in. `onToken`'s `prev.some(m => m.id === streamId)` therefore found a stale row where the fresh reply belonged and **replaced its content in place** instead of appending a new bubble; the stale row keeps its old timestamp, so the answer sorted far above the newest messages or hid behind a reset marker. Hydration from PB mints synthetic ids from 2,000,000 (`PB_SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE`), so a refresh could not collide — which is exactly why refresh was the only working cure. The tool-error variant is the same mechanism: a tool round emits no round-1 content tokens, so the round-2 answer vanished into the collided row. An earlier review fixed exactly this class for PB rows — `PB_SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` is a monotonic module counter precisely because a per-fetch index collided — and left localStorage rows and the per-load counter reset untouched.
  - **Fix — three guards, because rows only exist once they materialize.** `reseedOptimisticCounter` runs after hydration and is **monotonic** (`Math.max(msgCounter, max + 1)`, so an id already handed out this load — a reply row reserved before its first token — is never re-issued) and reads **only ids inside the optimistic range** (`typeof m.id === "number" && m.id < OPTIMISTIC_ID_CEILING`), so a hand-edited or migrated PB-space id seeds nothing and one string id cannot pin the counter to `NaN` via `Math.max`. The ceiling itself — `PB_SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE − 1_000` — is held at the **allocation seam**, not by the reseed: the reseed can legitimately place `msgCounter` AT the ceiling, so `nextOptimisticId()` saturates rather than crossing into PB space. A duplicate optimistic id is a visible glitch in an already-degenerate state; an id that collides with a PB row is the overwrite bug this scheme exists to prevent.
  - **The 5-minute watchdog never raced the body read, so a wedged stream bounced the dots forever.** `chat-stream.ts` now composes ONE signal — `AbortSignal.any([callerStop, AbortSignal.timeout(opts.watchdogMs ?? 300_000)])` — and drives **both** the fetch and the read-loop race. The distinction that matters is kept client-side: `failError(stopSignal)` builds the thrown error from the **caller's own** signal, so a watchdog expiry is never reported as a stop (the store renders "Stopped." whenever the caller's controller is aborted and never reads the error's name). A stalled `reader.cancel()` is released best-effort and deliberately **not** awaited — on the runtime that ignores the fetch abort on the body, which is the case this watchdog exists for, awaiting it would delay the honest error and re-introduce the hang. `watchdogMs` is the test seam.
  - **The route's honest error text was being thrown away.** The route answers `event: error` with the one actionable sentence it actually knows — *"My brain isn't configured yet — add a provider in Settings → AI Models."* — and the store's catch discarded it (`void error;`), substituting one of two hardcoded outage strings, so a tool error and a missing brain looked identical and unfixable. `RouteChatError` now carries that text and is identified by **`error.name === "RouteChatError"`** (a name check survives both module mocks and serialization, where `instanceof` would not). `send()` renders it verbatim, filters out the blank row the last attempt frame left behind, and keeps `errorFor` so **Try again** still works — retrying is the right move after a parent configures a provider. Real network failures keep the offline/server copy, decided on `navigator.onLine`.
  - **An empty `tool_call_id` 400'd the next round.** Several OpenAI-compatible gateways emit `tool_calls` stream deltas with no `id`; the route pushed `tool_call_id: ""`, and strict servers validate a `role:"tool"` message's id against the ids on the **preceding assistant entry**, so the turn died mid-loop and the user watched the dots go nowhere. `toolCallIdFor` synthesizes `call_<ts>_<i>_<rand>` **only when the provider sent nothing** (a real id is returned verbatim), and `normalizeToolCallIds` is now the single seam where the id and the `type` beside it are derived: it returns the very array echoed on the assistant message, defaults `type` to the only kind OpenAI defines (`"function"` — streamed deltas carry none), and the tool replies read their id back off that same array, so the two cannot drift.
  - **A stopped reply used to come back.** Pressing stop rendered "Stopped." while the route happily persisted the full answer; `ensureHydrated` + `mergeThread` then handed that answer back on the next hydrate, so the cancelled reply reappeared in the family thread on its own. The guard now reads **`request.signal.aborted` and `clientGone`** — both are inferences, not a cancel the route can await, so a requester that stops reading while its socket stays open is still missed, and a cancel message the route awaits remains the durable fix. `request.signal` is the **load-bearing** half: with the new 15s heartbeat a stop in the final ≤15s of a turn is unobserved by `clientGone`, because nothing tries to write after the answer is in. `ba03a4b` proves the aborted turn actually reached the persist decision rather than asserting it at a seam that never runs.
  - **New: a live reasoning transcript and per-tool activity chips, and both are display-only.** The route forwards every `reasoning_content` delta as `event: reasoning {"r"}` and wraps each tool execution in `event: tool {"name","state":"running"|"ok"|"error"}` (all `running` frames are written **before** any call runs; the state is `toolResultFailed(result)` — a result object carrying an `error` key). `ThinkingDisclosure` is open for the live think, auto-collapses when the answer's first token clears `isTyping`, and stays re-openable on the finished bubble; `ToolActivityChips` upsert **by name** (announce-then-result would otherwise show each tool twice) and render ⏳/✅/❌. The accessible state is an `sr-only` **word**, not `aria-label`: the chip is a bare `<span>` → implicit `generic`, for which ARIA 1.2 marks naming PROHIBITED. `state: "error"` covers several routine handler refusals, not crashes, and chip copy must never imply one. Stripping happens at the **single write seam** — `persistHistory()` maps every row through `stripVolatile`, rather than at each call site, because `setMessages` runs on every token tick and a per-site omission would put a whole think into localStorage and replay it on the next load as if Consuela had said it; `modelHistory` maps `role` + `content` only, so nothing goes back out to the model either.
  - **Displayed now equals persisted, on purpose.** The route persists only the answering round, so `event: attempt {"round","target"}` (the chain **index** `t0`/`t1`…, or the literal `"exhausted"` — never a model id, because this route sits on the middleware `API_EXEMPT` list and answers with no session) resets the content accumulator, the bubble and the transcript. The **tool activity is deliberately not per-attempt** — see the fix wave's finding 1. The key is **(round × target)**, not round alone, so a mid-round failover and the exhaustion fallback both supersede the attempt the user was watching. This is why behaviour change (1) below is a replacement rather than an append.
  - **New: a 15s SSE heartbeat + `X-Accel-Buffering: no`, and a 120s budget for streamed provider calls.** Silent reasoning used to read as a dead stream, and a buffering intermediary can accumulate frames and drop the final burst; the route now writes a `: ping\n\n` comment frame every `HEARTBEAT_MS = 15_000` (parsers drop comment-only frames) and sets the no-buffering header. `AI_STREAM_TIMEOUT_MS = 120_000` covers streamed calls, where a reasoning model re-planning after a tool error needed more than 60s; **the buffered and planner calls stay at `AI_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000`** — they are not the slow path, and widening them would only lengthen a hang.
  - **Seven new tools, plus the PIN chip for the eighth surface.** Kid-safe reads: `get_hall_of_fame` (newest week first, then podium order **within** a week — PB return order is not a ranking, so "who won last week?" could otherwise surface rank 2 first), `get_skill_tree`, `get_time_capsules`. Parent writes: `remove_grocery_item`, `remove_meal` (the Meals UI's real delete seam, sharing `resolveMealDay` with `add_meal` so both resolve a day the same way), `create_time_capsule`. Plus `propose_reward_redemption` — validate-and-return only, an inert `{tool:"redeem_reward"}` proposal generalized from the single point tool to a `PROPOSAL_TOOLS` map so it survives **buffered** exhaustion too — and `RedeemRewardChip`. The three kid reads go into **both** `KID_TOOL_NAMES` and `ai/KID.md` (the `write-ai-boot.mjs` drift gate enforces it) with rows in `ai/TOOLS.md` and `npm run ai:boot` regenerating `src/lib/ai-boot.generated.ts`.
  - **Chat never spends points.** The reward OWNER's PIN confirms the redemption, and a parent PIN is additionally required only when `cost > PARENT_APPROVAL_MIN_COST` (100, strictly greater, mirroring the route's own check — the two literals are pinned to each other by test so a change must be made in both files on purpose). The chip POSTs the canonical **`/api/rewards/redeem`** with the PINs in the JSON body, which re-reads the stored row for the real price and re-verifies both PINs server-side; deliberately **not** `/api/consuela/planner/apply`, whose `ALLOWED_TOOLS` may only ever create an event or apply a PIN-confirmed adjustment. `operationId` is the idempotency key and is **frozen at mount** (a retry re-reading a changing prop could spend the points twice); `409`/`reason:"duplicate"` renders as **done**; a `202` (`reconciled:false`) withholds the number until the ledger and the dashboard agree, because printing one number beside the other shows the family two balances for the same child. Two 401s are told apart — wrong PIN vs `parent_approval_required`.
  - **The tool registry's honesty contract: a read whose absence is meaningful must not answer `[]`.** A PocketBase outage behind the Hall of Fame, the skill catalog or the capsules returned an empty list, which reads to the model as a confident **no** — "you have none", "there are no quests yet", "you are not in the hall of fame" — and to a child, that is the answer they act on. Each now degrades with an explicit error that says **do not guess** (`hall of fame unavailable — do not guess who is enshrined`, `skill tree catalog unavailable — do not guess branches or quests`, `capsule data unavailable — do not guess what exists`), and the empty array is reserved for a genuinely empty result. `ai/KID.md` and `ai/TOOLS.md` teach the same distinction to the model. `liveSkillCatalog` reads PocketBase **directly** rather than through `skill-tree.ts`'s `getSkillBranches`/`getAllQuests`, because both `catch → []` — the same conflation one layer up.
  - **Three correctness calls made in review, worth keeping.** (1) `get_skill_tree` **never creates a row** and **never falls back** to the legacy `demo-user` profile: a missing member gets `zeroSkillTreeProfile` — the shape a brand-new member would have, never persisted — and a blank or explicitly-legacy id is refused *before* the query, because `sanitizeUserId` maps a blank id to that namespace. A child must never be shown another member's XP, and a read tool must never write. (2) Every member and hall emoji goes through **`textEmoji()`** (branch icons too): `members.emoji` and `hall_of_fame.emoji` hold either a glyph or a 100–250KB **photo-avatar data-URL**, and `get_leaderboard`'s comment records that payload as a verified live cause of the provider's "snag connecting to my brain" request-limit failure — one base64 avatar in a tool result is enough to blow the request limit. (3) `create_time_capsule` is created **empty** (`contentCount: 0`): reporting a count there would be counting rows nobody wrote, since contents are added on the Time Capsules page.
  - **Behaviour change 1 — a tool-heavy turn REPLACES its visible preamble instead of appending it.** "Let me check the grocery list…" is no longer left above the answer it led to, and its transcript is cleared with it. The alternative was a thread that contradicts what a refresh shows plus a dead round's reasoning floating above the answer; the blank is honest, the stale reasoning is not. This also applies to the transcript on a **mid-round failover**, and it is **mandated**, not an oversight: keeping a superseded attempt's reasoning next to words that were just retracted is fiction. The ✅/❌ chips are the exception and are **per-turn** (fix-wave finding 1): the tools that gathered the answer belong to the answer. Only the route's synthesized `exhausted` answer starts with a clean chip list.
  - **Behaviour change 2 — a 5-minute watchdog expiry also drops the answer, and this is a real loss.** The client composes the caller's stop with `AbortSignal.timeout(300_000)` into **one fetch signal**, so a watchdog kill closes the same socket and arrives at the route identically; the discriminator is client-side and the route cannot see it. The arithmetic does not fit: a legitimate streamed turn runs well past the 5-minute cap (a single-target chain's worst case is 6 rounds × 120s ≈ 12 minutes, and a failover chain crosses the cap *inside one round* at 3 targets — 3 × 120s > 300s — where 60s needed 6), and the cap grows with the configured fallback chain. A turn the watchdog kills reaches the family thread **from no device at all**; the only copy is the offline text on the requester. Dropping is still the lesser evil — resurrection was the bug this replaced — but it is a loss, and `AI_STREAM_TIMEOUT_MS`'s widening is what widened the window. Raising the client cap or lowering `AI_TIMEOUT_MS` is deliberately **left as a separate decision**, because the right value depends on the family's configured provider chain.
  - **Known condition 3 — health classification is deliberately NOT aligned with persistence.** `recordChatOutcome` still keys off `clientGone` alone, so a stop the persistence guard correctly catches is never recorded as `client_gone`: on the success path it lands as `ok`/`wrapup`, and if the aborted stream then throws it lands as `snag`. Folding `request.signal` into health would also reclassify the watchdog timeout away from `snag`, which is exactly the signal the health ring exists to surface. The two are intentionally decoupled; **flagged for whoever owns the health ring** (`src/lib/ai/health.ts`), because Settings → AI Models → AI Health now under-reports `client_gone` by design.
  - **Known condition 4 — a stop after the last token but before the client has processed `[DONE]` drops an answer the user saw in full.** With the heartbeat, a stop in the final ≤15s is invisible to `clientGone`, and `request.signal.aborted` catches it — so the bubble shows the complete answer, it survives in **localStorage**, and it never reaches the server thread. The other devices never see it. Correct per the resurrection fix, but the user is looking at a reply that exists nowhere else. **The bound is "before the client processes `[DONE]`", not "before the server writes it"** — and that is only true because `chat-store` now drops its abort controller the moment `streamConsuelaChat` resolves (see the fix-wave-review wave below). Between `73721c1` and that fix, this condition was understated: the window also covered the terminator's flight time, the store's post-stream reconcile round trip and the two PB inserts, so a stop pressed with the finished answer on screen silently deleted it from the family thread.
  - **Known condition 5 — a capsule created from chat is private by default, matching the UI form — but the tool takes no `recipients` parameter.** "Make a capsule for the twins" therefore yields a capsule only the creating parent can see, unless the model passes `isFamilyWide: true` explicitly. Both paths state the consequence: the tool description, the create form's own default and `ai/TOOLS.md` all say private means private until recipients are picked on the page, and a stringly-typed `false` no longer widens visibility (`996074b`). The model is told never to widen visibility to be helpful.
  - **Verified at the fix-wave head:** full Vitest **4976/4976 (479 files, exit 0)**; `tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0); targeted ESLint 0 findings on every touched file. The Playwright probe `scripts/consuela/verify-chat-reliability.mjs` **exists and was executed — 37/37 checks passed**, run as `CHAT_RELIABILITY_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3111 node scripts/consuela/verify-chat-reliability.mjs`. Two honest limits on that number: (a) **its default boot path did not run** — another session's dev server (PID 98785) holds Next 16's per-directory lock, so the probe adopted that server instead of booting its own, which means `bootDevServer`/`waitForReady`/`stopDevServer` are still **unexercised** and the adopted server did **not** carry the probe's `buildProbeEnv`; (b) `/api/hermes/chat` is 307'd wholesale to the probe's loopback SSE server, so it verifies the **client half of the §5.7 wire contract only — no `route.ts` code executes under it.** The frame sequences it replays are transcribed from the route, which is why mutating the client (dropping `toolEvents` from `volatileFields()`) turns its settled-chip checks red.
  - **Waived gate — `scripts/consuela/verify-chat-speed.mjs` does not pass, and is PRE-EXISTING.** Measured **0/7** at this head, in two distinct ways: 5 runs where the Send button never becomes enabled (the probe fills once and clicks, and a dev-mode StrictMode remount right after the fill discards the controlled value, so the click times out and the script dies before printing a check), and 2 runs that rendered fine but missed the budget at **2142ms / 2199ms against a 2000ms** first-paint limit. Proven not ours by a control experiment: with this feature's probe file removed from the tree it failed **2/2** identically. Its green baseline was a **cold** dev server (924ms). Not fixed in this wave because `verify-chat-speed.mjs` is outside this wave's file scope; the fix is the 3-line fill-and-retry already used by `ask()` in `verify-chat-reliability.mjs`. Recorded here rather than left silent because the plan made it a gate.
  - **Still required as a manual post-deploy check** (the probe cannot cover the route half): send a message that asks for a tool and confirm the ✅/❌ chip appears **on the finished reply**; send one that triggers a tool **error** and confirm the ❌ chip is still there after the answer settles; **press stop mid-answer, then reload and confirm the answer does NOT come back.**
  - **Ops:** ordinary image redeploy. **No PB schema changes → no `npm run pb:seed` needed**, no migration, no dependency change, **no new env var and no new secret requirement.** Post-deploy, Settings → AI Models → AI Health is worth reading: it reports the live-chain outcome distribution this feature's classes now feed, and it is the only way to learn whether fallback providers exist behind the brain (the family could not say).
  - **Contracts:** (1) optimistic ids are allocated **only** through `nextOptimisticId()` and stay strictly below `PB_SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE`, saturating at the ceiling rather than crossing it — never re-seed from a PB-space or non-numeric id; (2) the caller's stop and the watchdog compose into **one** fetch signal, and only the caller's own signal may be reported as a stop; (3) an SSE `error` frame's text is rendered **verbatim** — the offline/server copy is reserved for real network failures; (4) a `role:"tool"` message's `tool_call_id` and its assistant entry's `type`/`id` are derived in `normalizeToolCallIds` together, and a missing provider id is filled, never trusted through as empty; (5) a client-side abort **that lands before the `[DONE]` terminator** suppresses persistence, so persistence and health classification are intentionally decoupled — a turn already terminated for the requester is stored, because "Stopped." is rendered only from the store's catch; (6) `reasoning` and `toolEvents` are display-only and are stripped at `persistHistory()`'s single seam; (7) an `attempt` frame supersedes the visible preamble and the reasoning transcript — displayed equals persisted; tool activity is per-TURN and only the `exhausted` attempt clears it; (8) a tool read whose absence is meaningful returns an explicit **do-not-guess** error, never an empty array; (9) chat never moves points or redeems a reward — only the PIN chip, and the server re-verifies.
  - **Final-review fix wave (4 Important findings, 4 commits).** The whole-branch review found four things the vitest suites and the probe had both waved through.
    - **(1) Tool activity chips never reached the finished message.** `route.ts` writes an `attempt` frame before **every** provider call — including the answering round — and `chat-store.ts` reset the turn's chips on every attempt. The answering round runs no tools, so nothing ever put them back: `volatileFields()` was always chip-less, `chat/page.tsx`'s finished-message chips were **unreachable in production**, and a ❌ tool error was visible for a few seconds and then erased with no evidence on the answer — the second reported symptom. The transcript stays per-**call** (a superseded attempt's reasoning must not sit next to words that were retracted); the chips are per-**turn**, and only the route's synthesized `exhausted` answer clears them, so "I ran out of steps" no longer inherits six rounds of ✅/❌ as if they described it. The live surfaces also moved from `isTyping` to `streaming`, because `isTyping` drops on the answer's first token while the finished bubble only takes the activity at finalize — a long answer would otherwise spend its whole duration with no chips anywhere. `7c1193e`.
    - **(2) Two tests were vacuous.** The test that "proved" the chips land on the finished message used a `pendingStream()` mock that **never called `onAttempt`**, so it asserted a frame order the route cannot produce. Both it and the store suite now emit the real order (attempt → that round's frames → the answering round's attempt → tokens), and `verify-chat-reliability.mjs`'s tool-error scenario gained the answering round's missing attempt frame plus three assertions on the **settled** bubble. All three are mutation-checked: dropping `toolEvents` from `volatileFields()` turns them red while the live-phase checks stay green.
    - **(3) The id-collision fix had a window left in it.** `reseedOptimisticCounter` ran **after** `await fetchPBThread()`, by which point the stale localStorage rows were merged *and rendered* and the composer was live for the whole function — a send landing in that await still allocated 101/102 onto rows that already held those ids, which is the reported bug verbatim. The reseed is monotonic and range-filtered, so running it immediately after the merge closes the window at no cost. `8ab84cf`.
    - **(4) Four high-traffic reads still answered `[]` / "no conflict" on a read failure** — the exact anti-pattern the entry's contract (8) declares. `get_todays_events` and `get_todays_schedule` answer a **kid**; `get_dashboard_summary` is what `ai/TOOLS.md` tells the model to call **first**; and `check_conflicts` is documented as a **precondition for `add_event`**, so a PocketBase outage made add_event skip a real double-booking on a false negative. `liveEvents`/`liveGoogleEvents`/`liveSchedules` now return `| null` for a failed read — the idiom the pantry, grocery, reward and capsule readers already used — and each call site emits the matching **do not guess** shape, with an `events_error` leg added to the summary beside its existing `meals_error`/`tasks_error`. `mergedTodaysEvents` returns null only when **both** calendar sources fail, matching `liveEventsRange`, so the two calendar surfaces agree. `8fa1827`.
    - **Two small correctness calls in the same pass.** The route fills an unauthenticated caller's name with the literal `"Guest"`, and `verifiedCallerName` accepted it — so a caller with no session got a convincing identity and a synthesized `{ member: "Guest", level: 1, totalXP: 0 }` profile for a member who does not exist. It is a placeholder, not a member, and now reads as no verified identity (`d20eed6`). Separately, the redemption's idempotency key deliberately shares the `task-op-…` namespace `applyWeekLedgerOperation` dedupes on, so the redeem route can replay it; that is now a comment, because the next reader would otherwise "tidy" it into a collision.
    - **A stop landing inside the persist await no longer resurrects the answer (M3 follow-up, now closed).** The guard read `request.signal.aborted` / `clientGone` *before* `await persistChatPair(...)`, so the last window — the await itself — was decided with signals read before it opened: a stop landing there stored the pair, and the next `ensureHydrated` + `mergeThread` handed the full answer back over the "Stopped." the requester was looking at. The exact mirror image of the resurrection this entry's `ba03a4b` closed, on the same code path. **Fixed by ordering, not by undoing:** `[DONE]` is now written BEFORE the store write, so a turn is **delivered to the requester before it is stored**. That is what closes the contradiction rather than merely shrinking the window — `chat-stream.ts` breaks its read loop on `[DONE]` and resolves normally, and `chat-store.ts` renders "Stopped." only from its **catch** (`controller.signal.aborted`), so a requester already holding the terminator cannot be put on the catch path by anything the server does afterwards. Any abort that lands *before* the terminator still persists nothing, unchanged. **Why not the re-check-and-delete:** the `chat_messages` surface is `insertChatMessage` / `selectChatMessages` and nothing else — there is no `deleteChatMessage` to call, so undoing the written row means adding one to `src/db/{index,pb-db}.ts`, and it is the worse semantics anyway: it would delete rows a second device may already have merged, and make "stored" depend on a network race rather than on what the requester was handed. **Disclosed semantic change:** `[DONE]` now means "this answer is complete and has been handed to the requester", not "the row is already in PocketBase" — the two are one or two PB round trips apart, and the promise is **conditional**: any client-side abort landing before the persist guard still suppresses the store. A **Clem** turn stores nothing at all (`!isClem` on the guard), so for that agent the terminator promises delivery and nothing more. Harmless for the shipped client (the post-stream reconcile is `mergeThread`, which is add-only, and the bubble is already in local state and localStorage), but it is a weaker guarantee than the previous ordering implied. **Residual, restated because the first statement of it was wrong:** this paragraph used to claim that an abort racing the terminator in flight "can still leave \"Stopped.\" over a stored pair", bounded by the frame's flight time. It cannot, and never could — an abort in that gap puts `chat-store` on its **catch**, which renders "Stopped.", and the same abort makes the route skip `persistChatPair`, so the bubble and the server thread agree; and an abort after the gap never reached the store at all, so it rendered no "Stopped." either. What that gap actually cost was the *opposite* failure, and it was invisible: the answer stayed on screen, nothing said "Stopped.", and the pair was never stored. `chat-store` now drops its abort controller the moment `streamConsuelaChat` resolves, so **no abort can reach the socket after the client processes `[DONE]` at all** — the frame's flight time, the reconcile round trip and the two PB inserts are out of the window. The residual loss is the narrower condition 4 above: a stop in the gap *before* the client processes `[DONE]`, where dropping is the correct reading of the user's intent. **Test:** the new case holds both inserts open and asserts the terminator reaches the requester *while the pair is half-written*, then aborts and asserts the stored pair is byte-identical to a connected turn's. **RED verified** against the pre-fix `route.ts` (`expected 'data: {"t":"lo"}\n\n' to contain 'data: [DONE]'` — the read ran out its deadline with the route still blocked inside the persist await). **No constant touched** (`AI_STREAM_TIMEOUT_MS`, `AI_TIMEOUT_MS`, the 300s client cap, `MAX_ROUNDS`, `HEARTBEAT_MS`) and **no change to the health/persistence signal split** — `recordChatOutcome` still keys off `clientGone` alone.
  - **Follow-ups recorded, NOT done here — deliberately out of this wave's scope.**
    - **`SyncInit` can wipe `consuela-chat-messages`.** The key has been in `DEMO_KEYS` since `e47b708`, so any device behind the data epoch loses the thread — and that thread now holds stopped replies, error rows, their **Try again** target and the inert PIN chips, none of which are persisted anywhere else. It cannot fire for a device that has run the epoch-2 build (`CURRENT_EPOCH` is still 2), so the blast radius today is near zero, but the semantics are wrong and this feature made them wronger. **Remove `consuela-chat-messages` from `DEMO_KEYS`.**
    - **`recordChatOutcome` under-reports `client_gone` by design** — health and persistence deliberately use different signals, and folding them together would reclassify the watchdog timeout away from `snag`. Already disclosed above; **do not change.**
    - **The client's 5-minute watchdog cap sits below a worst case that GROWS with the configured fallback chain** (`MAX_ROUNDS` × targets × 120s). A separate constants decision, deliberately not taken; two sessions share this checkout.
- 2026-10-02 — fix(tasks): a stranded task id can no longer make a chore undeletable — **Files:** `src/lib/task-utils.ts` (`mergeTasksSnapshot` re-key pass), `src/lib/task-operation-outbox.ts` (`strandedTaskId` + heal in `processEntry`), NEW `tests/unit/task-utils-stale-id-heal.test.ts` (9), NEW `tests/unit/task-operation-outbox-unknown-task-heal.test.ts` (5), `tests/unit/task-utils-snapshot-merge.test.ts` (one assertion corrected, see below), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.6, this changelog.
  - **The bug (reported as "couldn't be sent" on the Tasks screen).** `mergeTasksSnapshot` matches rows by id **or** by `(title, assignee)`. A server row matching a local row on that second key with a **different** id means the server re-keyed the chore — and `recurringClone()` issues each day-sweep recurrence clone with a NEW id and the same title/assignee (a lineage IS title+cadence+owner), with "↻ Repeat last week" and delete-then-re-add re-creating rows the same way. The merge suppressed the new row as "already known" and left the device on the OLD id forever. That stranded id is what every task command sends, so `liveSnapshotTasks()` did not contain it, `POST /api/tasks/manage` answered `404 unknown_task`, the outbox marked the entry `failed` (terminal — never retried), the **display-only** optimistic hide was released, and the row reappeared on the next 60s pull. Net effect: `/api/tasks/claim` 404'd the same way, the family could never delete or complete that chore from that device, and a permanent "N couldn't be sent." banner sat above the list. The 2026-10-01 recurrence work made it fire once per recurring chore per day, which is why it kept recurring.
  - **Fix 1 — the snapshot's id is authority.** The merge now **re-keys** a local row onto the server's id when the `(title, assignee)` key matches with a differing id. Deliberately narrow: only that key, and never onto a tombstoned id, never onto an id a different live local row already holds, and never from/to a non-integer id — validity is read from the **raw** snapshot row, because the `restored` mapping mints an id for a malformed one and trusting that would swap a stale-but-real id for a second invented one. Only the `id` changes, so a kid's un-landed completion/pending stamps survive for the existing proof gates to arbitrate; the re-run is idempotent.
  - **Fix 2 — a terminal `unknown_task` self-heals device-side.** `404 unknown_task` on a command carrying a usable `taskId` now tombstones the id **on that device** and acknowledges the entry instead of failing it forever, so an already-poisoned device repairs itself on the next tap instead of needing a re-sync. It invents no server state: the row cannot exist on the server, so there was nothing to delete. Narrow by construction — a named refusal (`unknown_task_owner`), a config leg with no `taskId`, and every 401/403/409/5xx keep their existing classification, and with no adoption seam the heal degrades to `adoption_unavailable` rather than reporting a success that never happened. **Honest trade-off:** a completion tap landing in this window is dropped rather than replayed; the chore reappears under its true id, uncompleted, so the state is visible and retryable instead of silently paid or silently lost.
  - **One existing assertion corrected, deliberately.** `task-utils-snapshot-merge.test.ts` — "never duplicates a task already present by id OR by (title + assignee)" asserted `tasksChanged === false` and array identity for a title+assignee match with a different server id, i.e. it pinned the stranded-id behaviour. Its dedupe intent is unchanged and still asserted (one row), and it now also asserts the healed id. Full suite **4787 passed (475 files)**, exit 0; `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted ESLint 0 on every touched file.
  - **Not touched:** no server route, no PB collection/field/index, no dependency, no auth or gate change — the fix is entirely in the client merge plus the outbox's failure classification. **Ops:** ordinary image redeploy, no `npm run pb:seed`, no migration. Existing ghosts need no repair script: fix 1 heals them on the next snapshot pull and fix 2 on the next tap.
