- 2026-10-01 — feat(tasks): real daily/weekday recurrence, one-time expiry, favorites, repeat-last-week and a calendar due picker — **Files:** NEW `src/lib/task-recurrence.ts`, `src/components/tasks/DueDatePicker.tsx`, tests `tests/unit/{task-expires-after,task-day-sweep-recurrence,task-day-sweep-expiry,task-config,tasks-templates-ui,tasks-repeat-week,due-date-picker}.test.ts(x)`; MODIFIED `src/lib/{task-day-sweep,task-week-rollover,task-config,task-config-client,task-operation-outbox,task-ledger-command,task-utils,due-date-utils,task-manage,task-projection-reconciler,snapshot-tasks,pb-seed}.ts`, `src/types/tasks.ts`, `src/app/tasks/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/tasks/config/route.ts`, tests `tests/unit/{task-week-rollover,task-config-route,task-manage-route,task-utils,task-utils-crew,tasks-pending-approval}.test.ts(x)`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.6, `docs/DESIGN.md`, this changelog. Commits `f70d5b2`+`b7710d4`+`dd0617e` (expiresAfterDays), `a97700b`+`9541e7c` (recurrence + weekend freeze), `e9a7194` (weekly missed), `d9a30c2`+`14be525`+`0a955e8` (expiry), `34fb81d` (archivedTasks), `347a2ce` (templates leg), `98f67e1`+`f2c1992` (favorites UI + fixes), `a17fd19` (repeat week), `55e880c`+`8431731` (due picker + a11y/fit).
  - **There is one recurrence writer now, and it is the sweep.** `regenerateRecurringOnTasks` (`src/lib/task-day-sweep.ts`) groups daily/weekday rows by `recurringLineage` — title + cadence + owner identity (`universal` / `crew:<size>` / `assigned:<member>`) — consumes every stale instance of a lineage (completed or merely missed: the day is the unit) and spawns exactly one clone due today. A lineage that already has a row due today never spawns twice, pending-approval rows are immune from consumption but still seed today's instance, and the client-side twin was deleted: no browser path regenerates recurrence, so no device can double-write.
  - **Weekday lineages freeze over weekends (adjudicated).** Sat/Sun is consume-nothing, spawn-nothing: Friday's row stays visible until Monday's sweep replaces it, so a weekend never tombstones an undone weekday chore and never spawns a Saturday "weekday" instance.
  - **The weekly rollover consumes missed instances too.** `resetRecurringTasksForWeek` previously only consumed `completedEarlier` rows; an instance from a past week that was merely missed (`dueWeek < current`) now counts as consumed, so a skipped weekly chore is replaced by the new week's instance instead of stacking up beside it.
  - **One-time expiry is a tombstone stage with a strict N+1 boundary.** `expiresAfterDays` (integer 1–30) rides the new one-time "Auto-remove if unfinished" `Stepper`; `cullExpiredTasksOnTasks` tombstones incomplete, non-pending, non-recurring rows whose local day is strictly past `due + N` — cull at N+1, never at N. Completed rows, pending-approval rows and recurring rows are immune, and a task with no due date never expires. The cull shares the sweep's single locked snapshot mutation and `deletedTaskIds`, so tombstone propagation and the reconciler are the same as any delete; `dd0617e` taught the field read to tolerate PB's `0`-for-null, and `0a955e8` made the immunity fixtures genuinely past-deadline so the suite cannot pass vacuously.
  - **Favorites are a config leg, prefill-only.** `taskTemplates` joins rewards/penalties/weekly-prizes as a snapshot-only LWW leg (`taskTemplates` + `taskTemplatesStamp`) written through `/api/tasks/config`. The Add sheet shows a Favorites row of chips when templates exist; tapping one prefills the whole form (mode, points, cadence, expiry) and **never creates a task** — "⭐ Save as favorite" is an explicit toggle. Writes are replace-with-full-list; the leg is absent until its first write, so `upsert`/`delete` against an absent leg is `422` (not a silent empty list), and removing a favorite writes the full remaining list. `f2c1992` fixed the create path so the compose sheet updates optimistically instead of waiting for the ack.
  - **Repeat last week replays the rollover's own archive.** Each week rollover now captures the closed week's completed one-off defs into `archivedTasks[previousWeekStart]` (title/points/category/priority/owner/crew, `crewCloseMode` carried), bounded to the newest 4 week keys; every other surface treats it as read-only. The Tasks header's "↻ Repeat last week (N)" opens a confirmation `Modal` listing those defs (each removable), and Confirm queues the remainder as new uncompleted chores due today through the normal add path.
  - **The due field is a calendar, not a text box.** NEW `DueDatePicker` puts preset chips (Today / Tomorrow / Fri / Next week — the same local-day `getISO.*` getters the board uses) beside a formatted-date button that opens a Monday-first month `Modal`; `getMonthGrid`/`monthLabel` moved into `due-date-utils`. Every emitted day is a local `YYYY-MM-DD` string, so no cell can write a UTC neighbour. `55e880c` + `8431731` added the value to the button's accessible name and `aria-pressed` to the selected cell, and fitted the 7-column grid at 360px.
  - **Adjudicated during implementation:** config writes mint their own `operationId` in `queueTaskCommandAndFlush`, and `writeTaskConfig` binds its ack listener to the minted id — the outbox is keyed by `operationId`, so the spec's shared-id pairing between a browser command and its server echo was unsatisfiable.
  - **Verified:** full Vitest **4644/4644 (469 files, exit 0)** at this docs head (`55e880c`'s head was 4643/4643 before the due-picker a11y fix); `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted eslint **0 findings** on every touched file; `next build` exit 0; `push-safe.sh` CLEAN (exit 0).
  - **Ops:** run `npm run pb:seed` once — it self-heals the `expiresAfterDays` number field on the live PocketBase `tasks` schema (additive, existing collections skipped); no env, secret or migration change.
  - **Contracts:** five new lines in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.6 (single recurrence writer, tombstone-only N+1 expiry, prefill-only LWW templates, local-day date math, rollover-owned bounded `archivedTasks`); UI record in `docs/DESIGN.md` (2026-10-01).
- 2026-10-01 — feat(music): a family music player — Home widget plus a full `/player` page, streaming YouTube Music ad-free server-side — **Files:** NEW `src/lib/media/{youtube,queue}.ts`, `src/hooks/{useMediaPlayer,useMediaSearch}.ts`, `src/components/music/MusicWidget.tsx`, `src/app/player/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/media/{search,stream}/route.ts`, NEW `tests/unit/{media-queue,media-contract}.test.ts`, NEW `tests/live/media-probe.test.ts`; MODIFIED `src/lib/layout-config.ts`, `src/lib/nav-items.ts`, `src/app/page.tsx`, `tests/unit/layout-config.test.ts`, `package.json`/`package-lock.json` (`youtubei.js`); this changelog.
  - **Two surfaces, one state machine, on purpose.** Home gets a **preview** widget (artwork, title/artist, prev/play/next, an expand chevron, and a link through to `/player`); `/player` owns **search, the scrubber, volume and the queue**. The widget deliberately has no search box, so there is never a question of which of two boxes is live. Both surfaces render the same decisions because both call the pure functions in `src/lib/media/queue.ts` — the widget can never disagree with the page.
  - **`/player` is a widget drill-down, not a dock cap.** It is in `EXEMPT_ROUTES` with a reason, exactly like `/suggestions` and `/ledger`: the dock is contractually **seven caps for every role**, and a music cap would be the eighth. `nav-items.test.ts` + `route-shell-contract.test.ts` both pass unchanged, which is the check that this stayed a drill-down.
  - **Where the design came from, and what I did not copy.** The interaction model is inspired by `SFG5453/Orchard` (a `PlayerBar` widget + a `FullscreenPlayer` page — the same split) and `yappologistic/Sung` (Material 3 mini-player + immersive player). **No code was taken from either.** Orchard is **AGPL-3.0-or-later**, and copying its Vue/Quasar components would have made this app a derivative — for a *hosted* dashboard that means offering source to every user who loads the page. Sung is MIT but is a C++/Qt Quick native Linux app with no HTTP surface: its `backendserver.cpp` is `Backend::setupServer()` (scrobbling), not a server, so "integrate Sung's backend" was never available as a service. Everything here is original React/Tailwind against this repo's own design system. **Sung has Jellyfin + Navidrome/Subsonic; it has no Plex** — see the roadmap line below.
  - **The YouTube integration is the interesting part, and it is not `youtubei.js` doing the work.** Its Innertube clients (WEB_REMIX, ANDROID_VR, IOS, TV, MWEB, WEB — all six tested) return audio-only itags **with neither `url` nor `signatureCipher`**, so `format.decipher()` throws `"No valid URL to decipher"`; YouTube gates those itags behind a proof-of-origin token. Minting one with `bgutils-js` + a jsdom BotGuard realm *does* work (verified a 116-char token), but injecting it via `Innertube.create({ po_token })` is rejected (`service_integrity_dimensions … Starting an object on a scalar field`), so it buys nothing. What works is a hand-rolled **ANDROID_VR `/youtubei/v1/player`** request, which returns plain signed URLs for itags 139/140 (m4a) and 249/251 (webm/opus) with no deciphering at all — the same approach Orchard uses in `electron/playback/playbackService.js`, reimplemented here for a Next.js server runtime. Search still goes through `youtubei.js` (`ClientType.MUSIC`), which works fine and needs no identity.
  - **itag preference is 140 → 251 → 139 → 249, and m4a leads deliberately.** webm/opus sounds better per bitrate, but iOS Safari cannot decode webm/opus in an `<audio>` element; m4a first is the safe default with webm still available. `pickAudioFormat` is exported and unit-tested for exactly this.
  - **The proxy is the security boundary, and it is tested as one.** `/api/media/stream` validates the id against `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{11}$/` before it reaches the player endpoint, caps the forwarded `Range` header at 128 bytes, forwards `Range` so `<audio>` seeking works, passes `Content-Range`/`Accept-Ranges` back, and sends `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. **The signed googlevideo URL is a bearer credential and the guest cookie mints it, so neither reaches the browser** — `tests/unit/media-contract.test.ts` asserts the client-side media files contain no `googlevideo`/`visitorData` reference, so a future "just point the audio element at the CDN" shortcut fails the suite. Both routes are session-guarded with `verifySession`, matching `/api/services/runtime`.
  - **googlevideo answers `HEAD` with 403 but honours `GET` + `Range`** — which is exactly what seeking does. So the live probe asserts a range fetch, not a HEAD.
  - **Three bugs the tests caught in my own state machine, fixed in the source rather than the assertions:** `step()` claimed `trackChanged: true` even when repeat-one kept the same index (which would have reloaded the stream out of sync with the audio); stopping at the end of the queue left `index` pointing at a stale track so the UI showed a paused song that was no longer playing; and `tick()` at end-of-track with repeat-one now restarts cleanly. All three are pinned in `tests/unit/media-queue.test.ts`.
  - **Widget registration touched all five contract points** in `layout-config.ts` (`WidgetId`, `ALL_WIDGETS`, `WIDGET_TIERS`, `WIDGET_SPANS`, both default orders) and the `page.tsx` switch. **Hidden on the wall/tablet by default** — a glanceable panel with no search and no queue would show three dead buttons — which keeps `home-first-fold.test.ts`'s **exactly-12-cells** assertion true (11 visible widgets, one of which is the 2-cell photos hero). It stays switchable in Home settings.
  - **One repo-wide token rule respected:** the widget tone is `var(--color-accent-violet)`, not a hex — `radius-and-color-tokens.test.ts` has a **raw-hex budget that may only shrink**, and the token already has a light-theme value.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` exit 0 with `/player`, `/api/media/search` and `/api/media/stream` all registered; full Vitest **4600/4600 (462 files, exit 0)**; eslint 0 errors on every touched file (2 `<img>` warnings only). Live probe against the real API: `SEARCH_COUNT 3`, `MIME audio/mp4`, `RANGE_STATUS 206 BYTES 4096`.
  - **Ops:** none — no PB, env, secret or migration change, and **no new secret is required** (guest access needs no API key). `youtubei.js` is a new runtime dependency; the player endpoint is an unofficial API and can break without warning, so the failure mode is a 502 with a readable message, not a crash.
  - **Contracts:** a media surface must reach playback through `/api/media/stream` and never a direct CDN URL; a route reachable only from a widget goes in `EXEMPT_ROUTES` with a reason rather than the dock manifest; a new Home widget must keep the wall at 12 cells (hide it there if it needs interaction).
  - **Not in this slice, deliberately:** casting to other speakers (AirPlay/Chromecast/Snapcast — a browser tab can only emit audio from its own device, so this needs a sender process on a LAN host; **`lib/media/youtube.ts` is the seam**), Plex (net-new against Plex's REST API — Sung has no Plex either), Jellyfin/Navidrome (portable from Sung's MIT `jellyfin.cpp`/`subsonic.cpp`), and cross-device playback sync (the clock is tab-local today; `queue.ts` is framework-free so the swap is contained).



- 2026-10-01 — fix(tasks): a deleted task's row leaves the moment the delete lands, not 60s later — **Files:** `src/lib/task-operation-outbox.ts`, `tests/unit/task-operation-outbox.test.ts`. The server's delete ack says `deleted: true` but carries no id, and `adoptTaskOutboxAcknowledgement` early-returned on it — so when the acknowledgment released the optimistic hide, the row came straight BACK (the local store still had it) until the next 60s snapshot pull applied the tombstone. On the live dashboard this read as "the task will not delete": the receipts show the same task deleted twice five seconds apart, the second click only possible because the row had reappeared. `acknowledge()` now names the tombstone (the entry's own `taskId`) and adoption writes the local tombstone + drops the row immediately; a later add-only merge can never resurrect it. New outbox case pins: a 200 delete ack → row gone from `loadTasks()`, id present in `loadDeletedTaskIds()`. Also fixed the suite's render-time reassignment (`react-hooks/globals`, pre-existing). Verified: outbox 87/87, page/crew/tombstone suites 48/48, eslint 0 on touched files; full-suite + tsc + build were clean of this change's errors (the concurrent photos/music WIP in the shared checkout is the only red).
- 2026-10-01 — fix(tasks): crew chips render photo avatars as images, never as base64 text — **Files:** `src/components/tasks/CrewTasksCard.tsx`, `tests/unit/tasks-sections.test.tsx`. The parent Crew card's member chip rendered `m.emoji` as raw text, but a member's `emoji` field holds either a glyph or a photo data-URL (the "letterings" bug class — see `EmojiText`), so a photo member's entire base64 avatar dumped into the chip on the live dashboard (reported with Emily's). The chip now routes through the canonical `EmojiText` renderer (photo → `SigmaImage` circle, glyph → text). Pre-existing since the 5.7 page split carried the raw line over from the old inline page; the crew-close chips made it visible. New `tasks-sections` case pins photo → `<img src=dataURL>`, no `data:image`/`base64` in `textContent`, name and ✓ done still render. Verified: full Vitest **4539/4539 (459 files, exit 0)**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, eslint clean.
- 2026-09-30 — feat(photos): the wall gets a living family photo hero, and the photo library gets a moderation lever — **Files:** NEW `src/db/features/photos.ts`, `src/lib/photos/{curate,resize}.ts`, `src/components/photos/{PhotosWidget,PhotoUploader,WallPhotoGrid}.tsx`, `src/app/photos/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/photos/{route,file/route,upload/route}.ts`, NEW `tests/unit/{photos-curate,photos-widget}.test.tsx`; MODIFIED `src/db/features/{index,migrate}.ts`, `src/lib/layout-config.ts`, `src/app/page.tsx`, `src/lib/nav-items.ts`, `src/components/ui/NavIcon.tsx`, `src/app/globals.css` (`.wall-photo-*`), `tests/unit/{layout-config,home-first-fold,nav-items,more-sheet,settings-home-section}.test.ts(x)`, `docs/DESIGN.md`, this changelog.
  - **Why a hero, and what it cost.** The wall's grid is 3 columns × 4 rows = 12 cells, measured this month at 1046px of grid on a 1920px canvas; the 13th cell forces a 5th row and ~118px of page overflow on a surface that must not scroll. `photos` is `row-span-2`, so it costs two cells and three widgets had to pay for it: `DEFAULT_LAYOUT.tablet` now hides `schedule` (overlaps Today's Events), `financeLedger` (parents-only, `/money-mountain` covers it) and `consuelaSuggestions` (ambient; the Ask tab is where suggestions get acted on). All three stay one tap away in Home settings. Ten 1×1 widgets plus the 2-cell hero is exactly 12. A NEW assertion in `home-first-fold.test.ts` derives the wall's cell count from `WIDGET_TIERS` rather than hard-coding a number, so the next spanning widget fails a test instead of overflowing a panel.
  - **A latent bug the change exposed.** `cloneDefaultLayout()` returned `hidden: []` for all three modes. Harmless while no default hid anything; with a tablet default that hides three widgets it meant a **first-run panel would come up at 15 cells** and scroll. It now clones each mode's own `hidden` list. Found by the test updated for the new default, not by inspection.
  - **The wall never talks to PocketBase.** The original design resolved PocketBase file URLs in the browser; that needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_PB_PUBLIC_URL` (the app's `NEXT_PUBLIC_PB_URL` is the Docker hostname `pocketbase:8090`, unresolvable from a panel), and in SDK 0.27 `pb.files.getToken()` mints a token scoped to whoever asks — from the wall that is an admin-scoped token sitting in the panel's DOM. Replaced with `GET /api/photos/file`, which looks the record up, verifies the `f` param against it rather than trusting it, and streams the bytes with the admin JWT as a **request header** (file tokens exist to stand in for an auth record, and `photos` has none). Same-origin URLs, nothing secret in HTML, and `Cache-Control: immutable` is honest because the PB filename — which changes on every re-upload — is the cache key.
  - **Rotation: burn-in against the motion budget.** A panel that holds one image for hours gets image retention; the wall's motion rule forbids infinite animation. The resolution is discrete rotation — a ~75 s timer fires one 600ms opacity crossfade, an event rather than a loop. The next photo is `decode()`d **before** it is composited, so a slow or broken file never flashes; a failed decode leaves the current photo standing. Pauses while `document.hidden`; `prefers-reduced-motion` swaps with no transition at all, since a hard cut carries no motion. The skeleton is a static fill on purpose — a shimmer is an infinite animation that would keep running on an always-on panel while PocketBase is down.
  - **Curation is the only real logic here, so it is pure and seeded.** `curateWallPhotos` orders a day deterministically from a `YYYY-M-D` seed (a 3am Next restart or a panel reload resumes the same sequence instead of reshuffling in front of the family), drops future-dated photos rather than previewing next year, caps each album per pass so one vacation can't own the wall, and lets that cap yield under scarcity rather than hand back a two-photo loop. Recently-shown ids are demoted **before** selection rather than re-sorted after, which is what lets a small library still fill its queue instead of emptying it. An "on this day" picture from an earlier year jumps the queue and is labelled.
  - **Two defects the tests caught in the implementation, not the spec.** (1) The album cap was silently defeated by a backfill that appended capped-out photos whenever the queue ran short; selection is now four ordered phases (fresh-capped → fresh-uncapped → repeat-capped → repeat-uncapped), which also makes "every unseen photo precedes every repeat" true by construction. (2) The rotation cursor started at index 0 — the photo already on screen — so the first rotation composited the same picture over itself (React's duplicate-key warning, and a wall that visibly does nothing for 75 seconds). The cursor now starts at 1 and `advance()` refuses to re-pick the visible id.
  - **Upload keeps both copies.** The browser resizes on-device (canvas → ~1600px JPEG, 2× the hero tile) and uploads that beside the untouched original, so the panel never pulls a phone-sized original and the container needs no image library or ffmpeg. A file the browser can't decode (iPhone HEIC in Chrome/Android) still archives and the widget serves `original`. `takenAt` uses `File.lastModified` — no EXIF reader was added for one field. `showOnWall` is the pull-one-photo-off lever: reversible, no PocketBase admin console, no redeploy; deleting stays a different decision.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full Vitest **4538/4538 (459 files, exit 0)** — new suites `photos-curate` (17) and `photos-widget` (8), plus the updated `layout-config` (35), `home-first-fold` (6), `nav-items` (15), `more-sheet` (8) and `settings-home-section` contracts; `warm-glass-contracts` forced the grid badge off `text-[10px]` onto the 12px type floor. Nav/icon contracts updated for the 7th "More…" row (`/photos`, new `photos` glyph).
  - **Ops:** run `npm run migrate:features` once — it creates the `photos` collection (additive; existing collections are skipped). The collection is admin-only (`listRule`/`viewRule` = null) by design: reads go through the server routes, which is why no PB env var changed. `/api/photos` sits behind the existing session-cookie middleware gate, so a signed-out panel that has auto-logged-out shows "Photos unavailable" rather than the family's pictures — deliberate for private content, and the one-line `API_EXEMPT` exemption is an owner call, not a default.
  - **Contracts:** the photos tile is the only Home widget rendered without `WidgetCard` and must stay cropped-by-box; the wall's visible tablet set stays at 12 cells; no wall `<img>` may point at PocketBase or carry a token; rotation stays a discrete finite event; `showOnWall` is the removal lever; an empty, unreachable or not-yet-migrated library renders its honest state and never invents an image.


- **2026-09-29 — feat(tasks): crew close modes + the daily sweep** — Crew tasks gain per-task close modes: `strict` (today, default), `parent` (a parent can close with whatever check-ins exist), `deadline` (the new `ensureCurrentTaskDay` sweep — one locked snapshot mutation on the sync GET, idempotent per local day, marker `lastDaySweep` — closes it the morning after the due date). Partial closes stage a normal crew pendingApproval whose `crew` list is now the **authoritative award list** (`resolvePayees` validates membership instead of recomputing full-crew participation); approve / approve-all / send-back / undo are unchanged. Zero-check-in deadline crews just go overdue; pendingApproval rows are immune to the sweep. New `crew-close` claim-route command (parent PIN, receipts, idempotent per operationId). New UI: Add/Edit "Close when" segmented control (Everyone / Parent closes / At due date), Crew card mode chips + "Close with check-ins (N of M)" button, partial-count copy in the Needs-approval queue. Fields: `Task.crewCloseMode`; PB `tasks.crewCloseMode` via pb:seed self-heal. Contracts: 6 new lines in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §5.6. Tests: task-crew-close-mode, task-day-sweep, task-crew-close, tasks-crew-approve award-list cases, tasks-crew-close-flow, sync-route daysweep cases — full suite green, tsc clean, build clean, push-safe CLEAN. The old approval `skipped` formula (replay dedup arithmetic — `Math.max(0, entries.length - paid)` with its `duplicate`/`replayOnly` special cases) is replaced by award-list skips: `skipped` counts stored-award-list members missing from the current roster, so a replay may legitimately echo `skipped: N, paid: 0`, and `skipped` is a property of the stored award list + current roster, never of the mutation.

- 2026-09-29 — cleanup: 11 unwired modules deleted, and the cascade they created was measured rather than assumed — **Files:** DELETED `src/modes/adult/AdultHome.tsx` (732), `src/modes/family/FamilyHome.tsx` (246), `src/hooks/useOpenClaw.ts` (175), `src/components/conflicts/ConflictWarning.tsx` (158), `src/hooks/useWidgetTheme.ts` (124), `src/components/ui/ModeTransition.tsx` (65), `src/components/ui/SigmaAvatar.tsx` (55), `src/components/ui/PetAvatar.tsx` (39), plus the second wave below: `src/components/integrations/ProductSearchWidget.tsx` (189), `src/components/integrations/TravelTimeCard.tsx` (97), `src/components/ui/InstacartButton.tsx` (108); `src/modes/modes.css` (stale note), NEW `tests/unit/dead-module-contract.test.ts` (3), this changelog.
  - **What was dead.** Eleven files had **zero importers anywhere** — `src/`, `tests/` and `scripts/` all checked, not just `src/` — so they were compiled, type-checked and linted on every run while rendering nothing. `AdultHome` and `FamilyHome` are whole Home screens that lost their mount in `0f5bb1e` (2026-08-04) and have been unreachable since; `modes.css` had been carrying the note *"FamilyHome/AdultHome remain unwired"* as if it were current fact, which is why this looked deliberate rather than abandoned. Owner decision 2026-09-29: delete all eight, following the Phase 5.1 precedent that removed the dead stylesheets.
  - **The deletion cascaded — the part a single-pass check would have missed.** Re-running the reference scan *after* the first eight came back with **three orphans that had not been unreferenced before**: `ProductSearchWidget`, `TravelTimeCard` and `InstacartButton` were imported **only** by the `AdultHome` just removed, so removing it orphaned them (394 lines). Verified against the pre-deletion tree before deleting — a same-class claim, not an assumption. **1,988 lines removed across two waves.**
  - **The scan itself was wrong twice before it was right, and that is the useful part.** The first version mixed absolute and relative paths and reported 55 "dead" files including `tasks/page.tsx`, `CapsuleNav.tsx` and `pb-seed.ts`. The second fixed the paths but still produced false positives of three distinct kinds: barrel `index.ts` files that exist to be a public entry point; modules referenced only from `tests/`/`scripts/` (`RewardSection` has 5 references and 0 importers, `action-runner` 3 and 0) — *unused by the product* is not the same claim as *safe to delete*; and dynamic-import shapes the resolver must guess at.
  - **The contract is therefore targeted, not general.** NEW `tests/unit/dead-module-contract.test.ts` (3) is the B2 shape — `existsSync(...) === false` for each deleted path, a reason on every entry, a sweep proving no surviving `from "…/<name>"` import crept back into `src/`, `tests/` or `scripts/`, and a check that `modes.css` no longer names deleted files as current state. A general unreferenced-file guard was **not** shipped: with the false-positive classes above it would be a recurring nuisance, and its failure mode is pushing someone to delete working code.
  - **Left alone on purpose, needing a decision rather than a regex:** `hooks/useSafeFetch.ts` (180) has a real dedicated test suite and no product caller — it is *ready to wire*, not dead, so deleting it would also delete the test. `modes/adult/SwipeableRow.tsx` (156) is an orphan but is the same component name as the live `components/ui/SwipeableRow.tsx`, so the call sites want reading, not deleting. `ui/MemberModal.tsx` (262), `db/{migrate,schema,seed-emergency}.ts` and `db/features/seed.ts` are likely script-driven (`pb:seed`, migrations) and are referenced from tooling rather than the app. Roughly 2,500 lines remain in that category for a deliberate per-file audit.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` exit 0; full Vitest **4454/4454 (453 files, exit 0)**; `push-safe.sh` CLEAN (exit 0). No test was deleted and no `EXEMPT_ROUTES`, allowlist or `SHELL_EXEMPT` entry had to be removed — nothing referenced these files, which is the premise of the whole change.
  - **Ops:** none — no PB, env, secret, migration or `pb:seed` change. Unreachable code only; every route, API and surface that rendered before still renders.
  - **Contracts:** a module with **no importer in `src/`, `tests/` or `scripts/`** is dead and stays deleted (`dead-module-contract.test.ts`); `modes.css` describes the modes that exist, not retired screens. Deleting an orphan can orphan its own dependents — **re-run the reference scan afterwards**, and treat a new result as a cascade to verify, not a fresh finding. "No importer in `src/`" alone is not sufficient grounds: check `tests/` and `scripts/` first, and never act on a whole-tree scan that has not been checked for barrels and script-only consumers.

- 2026-09-29 — cleanup: the tap-target debt is 3 not 11, the kid's race line renders once, `/memory` is no longer a dead end, and the nav doc matches the manifest — **Files:** `src/components/{settings/RewardSection,money-mountain/CreateMountainForm,meals/RecipeBox,meals/RecipeModal,meals/RecipeImportModal,meals/RecipeSearchModal}.tsx`, `src/modes/kid/KidHome.tsx`, `src/app/memory/page.tsx`, `tests/unit/{tap-target-contract,kid-points-cards,route-shell-contract}`, `docs/DESIGN.md`, this changelog.
  - **The tap-target allowlist I wrote last turn was wrong, and the fix is better than the excuse.** It carried 7 files / 9 buttons as "documented debt" on overlap grounds. Re-measuring each one showed **three of those reasons were false**:
    - `RewardSection.tsx:238` and `CreateMountainForm.tsx:195` are 40px swatches in a `flex-wrap gap-2` grid (8px gap). A 44px box extends 2px per side against a 4px half-gap — **no overlap**. Both now carry `.hit-44`.
    - `PhotoInputButton.tsx:124` was never a violation: it already meets 44px by the rule's *other* permitted mechanism, 24px + `before:-inset-2.5` (2×10px) = 44px. It stays allowlisted only because the class string carries no `hit-44`, and the reason now says so with the arithmetic instead of hand-waving about "the target is the whole preview".
    - The genuinely blocked pair is the two emoji pickers in a `grid-cols-8 gap-1` (4px gap): a 44px box on a 32px cell extends 6px per side against a 2px half-gap, so the cells would **overlap**. Meeting 44px there needs a wider grid — a design change, not a class. Those two, and only those, remain allowlisted, with the geometry written down.
    Net: **11 offenders → 3**; buttons carrying `hit-44` **7 → 15**. The suite's stale-entry guard now proves the list is exactly the real remainder.
  - **`3cf9fb7`'s title was accurate about the wording and wrong about the screen.** It de-duplicated the race-line *templates* (both cards now call `kidRaceLine`) but left both *renders*, so a kid with weekly prizes configured read the same sentence twice on one page — once under the big week total, once under the leaderboard, which already states their rank ("You're #2 — you can do it!"). The leaderboard copy is removed; the hero week card owns the line, and `useWeeklyPrizes`/`kidRaceLine` stay imported for it. The old test asserted `weekCard.textContent` **contains** `boardLine.textContent` — it *pinned the duplication as intended*. Replaced with a behavioural pin: the line renders **exactly once**, inside `[data-testid="kid-week-card"]`, and `[data-testid="kid-prize-race-line"]` is gone from the DOM.
  - **`/memory` was a genuine dead end.** It rendered a bare `min-h-screen bg-background` div — no `PageShell`, so no dock, no sync banner and the wrong background token — with no back control, while Phase 3's `More…` sheet put "Family Memory" in front of parents. It now renders through `PageShell` with a `← Back to Home` link, and takes its id from `MEMORY_FAMILY_ID` instead of re-stringing the literal (`memory-ids.ts`: "import these, never re-string them"). Worth recording: `demo-family` looks like a placeholder but is the **real** family id for this deployment, so `/analytics` and the analytics routes were never broken and were left alone.
  - **New contract: `tests/unit/route-shell-contract.test.ts` (4)** — the dead-end counterpart to `nav-items.test.ts`'s no-orphan walk. Every `src/app/**/page.tsx` must *reach* `PageShell` (depth-capped transitive walk over `@/…` imports) or hold an `SHELL_EXEMPT` entry with a written reason; stale exemptions fail.
    - **It follows imports rather than grepping one file, and that mattered.** The six `/settings/*` routes delegate to `SettingsSectionView`, which owns the shell. A single-file scan reported them as seven dead ends — a false positive that would have pushed someone to "fix" correct code.
    - **It strips comments before matching, and that mattered too.** `Modal.tsx:105` explains that it portals to `<body>` "because rendered inline the overlay inherits PageShell's clipping", and `Modal` is transitively imported by the design-system page — so a bare `\bPageShell\b` search made that route look shell-covered and wrongly flagged a live exemption as stale. Two false-positive classes, both caught only by running the test rather than reasoning about it.
    - Exemptions, each with a reason: `design-system` (dev-only, production-gated), `grocery` (a pure `redirect()`, renders no UI), `screensaver` (the sleeping wall board), `meals/archive` (already carries `aria-label="Back to meal planner"`, so not a dead end — giving it the dock too is a UX decision, tracked not dropped), `settings/[section]` (calls `notFound()` and returns null).
  - **`docs/DESIGN.md` §1.1 rewritten against the manifest** — it still described 6 kid tabs, omitted Rewards, and predated the `More…` sheet. It now carries both tables (8 primary entries with role/wall columns, 6 secondary) plus the dead-end contract. **I verified the counts against the real `navItemsForRole`/`moreNavItemsForRole` instead of trusting my reading, and my first draft was wrong**: I wrote "8 caps to a parent, 7 to a kid, 6 on the wall". The truth is **seven for every role** — the manifest holds eight primary entries but `Rewards` is `KID_ROLES` and `House` is `guest`+`parent`, so exactly one of the pair is always present. That is precisely the invariant `CapsuleNav` sizes its capsule for. The `More…` sheet is 6 to a parent, 5 to a kid, 4 on the wall.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted ESLint 0 errors (5 pre-existing `no-img-element` warnings in the recipe sheets, untouched); `next build` exit 0; full Vitest **4451/4451 (452 files, exit 0)**; `push-safe.sh` CLEAN (exit 0).
  - **Ops:** none — no PB, env, secret, migration or `pb:seed` change. One `data-testid` removed (`kid-prize-race-line`) and its test rewritten.
  - **Contracts:** the dock is the only nav and appears on **every** route — a parent-reachable destination that renders no `PageShell` is a dead end and fails the walk. The leaderboard card owns the standings and the viewer's rank; the hero week card owns the prize-race line, and the line renders once. Sub-44 `<button>`s carry `.hit-44`, satisfy the rule's `before:/after:` inset alternative, or hold a geometric allowlist reason.

- 2026-09-29 — fix(tasks): close the UTC/local day sweep — due presets, the kid's "Done today", and the analytics overdue count — **Files:** NEW `src/lib/due-date-utils.ts` (+ `src/app/tasks/page.tsx` now imports it), `src/lib/local-date.ts` (NEW `localDateOfStoredDate`), `src/lib/task-utils.ts` (`getThisWeeksCompletedTasks`), `src/modes/kid/KidHome.tsx:391`, `src/lib/schedule-analytics.ts` (two sites), `src/modes/adult/AdultHome.tsx:151` (dead code), NEW `tests/unit/{due-date-local-days,this-week-completed-tasks-local-day,kid-home-done-today,schedule-analytics-overdue}` (22), this changelog.
  - **Scope.** This closes the sweep that the `539999d` changelog entry listed as "deliberately NOT fixed" (four named sibling sites) plus the two it missed. Every site was re-investigated rather than assumed: **four were real, two were not**, and the two false ones are recorded below so nobody "fixes" them later.
  - **(A) Due-date presets and the 31-day due list** — `src/app/tasks/page.tsx`. `isoOffset` was `new Date(Date.now() + days * 86400000)` serialized with `toISOString()`: a UTC date. **Two** defects, not one. (1) *Evening skew* — from 20:00 Detroit the UTC date has rolled over, so `getISO.tomorrow` was **two** days out, `thisWeek` ran to +7, every weekday quick-add was a day ahead, and the 31-row due `<select>` started on tomorrow. A chore filed under the wrong day then reads as overdue, or not-due-until-tomorrow, on the board. `getISO.today` was already local, so the preset row was internally inconsistent: "Today" right, everything beside it a day ahead. (2) *DST fall-back collision* — `+ 86400000` is exactly 24 hours, not one calendar day, so on the night the clocks go back 20:30 Monday + 24h is 19:30 Monday **local** and `isoOffset(1)` returned the **same date as** `isoOffset(0)`: "Tomorrow" became "Today". Fixed with `setDate` local-calendar arithmetic, which moves exactly one day across a 23- or 25-hour day. The helpers moved verbatim to NEW `src/lib/due-date-utils.ts` (the "single time-math source" idiom already used for `parseMinutes` and `weekStartForDate`) because they were module-private inside a 2,825-line page and therefore untestable; they are moved, not rewritten, and `getISO`'s getters still re-evaluate per read so "Today" cannot freeze at import time.
  - **(B) The kid's "✅ Done today" card** — `KidHome.tsx:391`. It compared `t.completedAt?.slice(0,10) === new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — UTC against UTC, self-consistent but semantically wrong, since the card means the **local** day. From 20:00 local a chore the kid finished at 3pm stopped appearing and the points total fell with it; the mirror case put a Sunday-night chore on Monday's card.
  - **(B2) `getThisWeeksCompletedTasks`** — found while testing (B), on the same path. Its unstamped branch compared a **full ISO instant** to **date-only local strings**: `t.completedAt <= now` is lexically false for *any* completion made on the current day, so a task with no `completedInWeek` stamp never counted as this week's — on **both** feeds (`tasks/page.tsx:1502` board filter and the kid card). The `completedInWeek === monday` fast path masked it for freshly-stamped rows, which is why it was easy to miss.
  - **(C) Analytics "overdue"** — `src/lib/schedule-analytics.ts`, twice (the total and the per-member breakdown repeated the expression verbatim). `new Date(t.due) < new Date()` parsed a bare local date as UTC midnight — the previous local evening in Detroit — so **from 20:00 local every task due *tomorrow* read as overdue**, and a task due *today* read as overdue for the entire day. The family had missed nothing and the count said otherwise. Now `isOverdue` asks "is that day before today".
  - **(D) `AdultHome.tsx:151` forecast weekday** — real but **currently unreachable**: `src/modes/adult/AdultHome.tsx` has had **zero importers since `0f5bb1e` (2026-08-04)** and is dead code (`modes/modes.css:9` says so in-tree). `days[new Date(d).getDay()]` named every open-meteo forecast day one early behind UTC. Fixed via `weekdayOfISO` (local noon) for correctness should the screen ever be re-wired; **no user-visible effect today**.
  - **Investigated and found NOT defective — do not "fix" these.** `skill-tree.ts:200-201` looks like the same class (`new Date(profile.lastActivityDate).toDateString()`) but is **local-to-local**: `lastActivityDate` is always written as a full ISO instant (`skill-tree.ts:29,223`) and `toDateString()` renders in local time, so the axes already agree. The `539999d` entry listed it as a sibling defect; it is not one. Likewise the six `weekStart` validators (`ledger-operations:153`, `task-approval:246`, `task-projection-reconciler:69`, `task-week-rollover:45`, `task-manage:257`, `hall-of-fame-backfill:54`) build `new Date(\`${v}T00:00:00.000Z\`)` and compare `toISOString().slice(0,10)` back — **both sides explicitly UTC**, so they are format round-trips with no axis to mix.
  - **A deliberate non-change.** `localDateOfStoredDate` returns `null` for the legacy `due` labels still written by `pb-db.ts:236` and `db/index.ts:142` (`"Today"`, `"Tomorrow"`, `"Later"` — note `"Later"` is not even in `migrateDueToISO`'s map). `new Date("Today")` is an Invalid Date and `NaN < x` is false, so those rows were **never** counted overdue. Promoting unparseable labels to "overdue" would inflate every count the moment it lands, so the conservative behaviour is preserved on purpose and pinned by a test.
  - **Tests (TDD, RED first, one suite per site).** `due-date-local-days` (8) — **RED 7 failed / 1 passed**, the green being `today` (already local, kept as a control); the DST case reproduced the collision directly (`expected '2026-10-28' to be '2026-10-27'`). `this-week-completed-tasks-local-day` (5) — **RED 3 failed** (`expected [] to deeply equal [ 'Trash' ]`). `kid-home-done-today` (3, reusing the `kid-home-race-line` harness with `task-utils`/`local-date` left real) — **RED 1 failed / 2 passed**: *"the 3pm chore must not vanish at 9pm"*. `schedule-analytics-overdue` (6, mocked `getAuthedPB`) — **RED 4 failed**; the mixed set counted **5** overdue where 3 were genuinely late. All four green, **22 total**.
  - **Two of the RED failures were bugs in my own tests, worth recording.** A helper doing `new Date("2026-10-29")` to compute expected dates reproduced the exact UTC-midnight defect it was meant to catch (fixed by parsing at local noon), and a `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual` on `YYYY-MM-DD` strings is a numeric matcher that threw on strings. The DST case had to be verified against `localWeekStartISO()` before the suite could distinguish a real regression from a wrong expectation.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted ESLint 0; `next build` exit 0; full Vitest **4447/4447 (451 files, exit 0)**; `push-safe.sh` CLEAN (exit 0).
  - **Ops:** none — no PB collection, field, index, env, secret, migration or `pb:seed` change. Every fix is display-side day math plus its test; **no stored `completedAt`, `due` or `lastActivityDate` value is rewritten**, so no data repair is required and existing rows are corrected on read.
  - **Contracts:** a `YYYY-MM-DD` string is a **local** calendar day and must never be parsed with `new Date(value)` (that is UTC midnight — the previous local day behind UTC); read it at local noon via `localDateOfStoredDate`. A `completedAt` / full ISO string is an **instant**; convert it with `localDateOf` before any day comparison. "Overdue" means the due **day** is before **today**, never that an instant is in the past. Date arithmetic advances with `setDate`, never `+ 86400000`. The remaining UTC-looking sites in `src/` are validators with both sides UTC-anchored — leave them alone.

- 2026-09-29 — fix(tasks): a completion's calendar day is the LOCAL day it happened on (the evening-streak half of `539999d`) — **Files:** `src/lib/local-date.ts` (NEW `localDateOf`), `src/lib/task-utils.ts` (two comparison sites), NEW `tests/unit/streak-completion-local-day.test.ts` (6), this changelog.
  - **The bug, and why `539999d` did not close it.** `539999d` (2026-09-28) correctly moved the week **cursor** to the local week via `weekStartForDate`, and its own comment claims the "streak reads 0 every evening 8pm–midnight" symptom is now gone. It is not: the commit left the **comparison** on the UTC axis. There are **two** such sites, not one:
    - `getThisWeeksCompletedDates` filtered `d.slice(0, 10) >= monday && d.slice(0, 10) <= now`, where `d` is a full ISO timestamp. Every writer produces `completedAt` with `new Date().toISOString()` (`task-claim.ts:1140`, `AdultHome.tsx:367`, `action-runner.ts:154`, `skill-tree.ts:174`) — a **UTC instant** — so `slice(0,10)` is the **UTC** date, compared against the **local** `monday`/`now`.
    - `calculateRealStreak` then matched `d.split("T")[0] === cursor` — the UTC date again — against a local `cursor`. So fixing only the filter would have admitted the row and then failed to count it: the streak would still read 0.
  - **It cuts both ways, and the second direction is worse.** In `America/Detroit` (the family's zone, pinned by `vitest.config.ts`) a completion made **20:00–24:00 local** serializes as *tomorrow's* UTC date: it fails `day <= now` and is **dropped from the week**, so the flame goes out through the evening — exactly when a family finishes its chores. But a **Sunday-evening** completion serializes as *Monday* and was **pulled INTO the new week**, inflating Monday's streak with a chore that belonged to the week that closed. The `539999d` changelog enumerated four sibling sites it deliberately left and named none of these, one of which sits in the very function it edited. Ahead of UTC (`Asia/Tokyo`) the mirror window is 00:00–09:00 local, where a Monday-morning chore reads as the preceding Sunday and is dropped.
  - **Why the existing suite stayed green.** `streak-week-scope-tz.test.ts` stamps every fixture `${iso}T10:00:00.000Z` — **06:00 Detroit / 19:00 Tokyo**, the middle of the day in both zones, precisely where the UTC date and the local date agree. The blind spot was in the fixture design, not the assertions. This was also flagged in review: the commit read as complete while its own stated symptom was fully present on the half it left untouched.
  - **The fix.** NEW `localDateOf(instantIso)` in `src/lib/local-date.ts` — the **local** calendar date of a full ISO instant, via the existing `localTodayISO`/`familyTimeZone` pair. Both comparison sites now derive the day in the family timezone before comparing local-to-local, so the axes can no longer disagree. No signature, return-shape or caller changed: every production caller already passes `getThisWeeksCompletedDates` output, and every test caller passes full `T…Z` instants, so no date-only string is ever parsed as an instant here.
  - **Tests (TDD, RED first).** NEW `tests/unit/streak-completion-local-day.test.ts` (6). **RED: 5 failed / 1 passed** — the lone green case is the control (a Tokyo Monday-evening instant read as Sunday is correctly excluded). The failures: Monday 20:30 local dropped (`expected [] to deeply equal [ '2026-09-29T00:30:00.000Z' ]`); a three-day evening streak collapsing to two; a Sunday-evening chore landing in the **new** week (`expected [ '2026-09-28T01:00:00.000Z' ] to deeply equal []` — the inflation direction); the same instant missing from the week it actually belonged to; and a Tokyo Monday 08:00 local dropped. **GREEN: 6/6**, with `streak-week-scope-tz` (7), `calculate-real-streak-tz` (4) and `task-utils` (15) unchanged and green — 32 together, no regression on the week-scoping behaviour `539999d` did fix.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted ESLint 0; `next build` exit 0; full Vitest **4425/4425 (447 files, exit 0)**. Secrets gate `push-safe.sh` CLEAN (exit 0).
  - **Ops:** none — no PB collection, field, index, env, secret or deploy change. The correction is display-side day math plus its test; no stored `completedAt` is rewritten.
  - **Contracts:** a `completedAt` is a **UTC instant** and a calendar key (`today`, `weekStartForDate(today)`, `due`) is a **local date** — never compare a `slice(0,10)`/`split("T")[0]` of an instant against a local key. Convert the instant first (`localDateOf`) or use `localTodayISO(new Date(iso))`. This applies to **every** site in the money/points path, not just the two fixed here; the sweep in the `539999d` entry (`tasks/page.tsx:84-86,135-136`, `KidHome.tsx:401`, `AdultHome.tsx:154`, `schedule-analytics.ts:340,357`, `skill-tree.ts:201`) is still open and is a real follow-up.

- 2026-09-29 — fix(a11y): the 44px tap-target floor, and a contract that keeps it — **Files:** `src/components/tasks/CrewTasksCard.tsx`, `src/components/leaderboard/DailyQuestCard.tsx`, NEW `tests/unit/tap-target-contract.test.ts` (3), this changelog.
  - **What `7b6ba5a` got wrong.** It raised two sub-24px controls citing WCAG **2.5.8 Target Size (Minimum)**, which is 24×24 at Level AA — the citation was factually right, and the level chosen was **below the repo's own rule**. `AGENTS.md` ("UI Contracts", a hard rule) requires **44×44**, with a compact visual permitted only when the control carries `.hit-44`. Neither did, and neither landed at 44: `CrewTasksCard.tsx:40` was `h-6 w-6` (**24px**) and `DailyQuestCard.tsx:39` was `min-h-8` (**32px**). The commit also shipped with no test at all, in a series where every other fix added a named contract.
  - **The fix.** `.hit-44` added to both, which is the mechanism the house rule prescribes: `globals.css:607` defines `.hit-44::before` as a centred `width/height: max(100%, 44px)` pseudo box — a 44px hit area with **no layout change**, so a 24px ✕ stays visually compact and becomes comfortably tappable. On the crew ✕ the centred 44px box extends ~10px past the chip, and crew chips sit 8px apart (`gap-2`), so adjacent hit regions overlap slightly and the later element in DOM order wins the overlap — the accepted trade-off of the pseudo box, and no change to the rendered layout.
  - **Scope correction — the real gap is 11, not 2.** A source scan of every `<button>` in `src/**/*.tsx` found **11** sub-44px controls with no `hit-44`; `7b6ba5a` fixed 2 and reported the standard met. The 7 remaining **files** (9 buttons) are allowlisted in the new contract with a written reason each, so the debt is now visible and bounded instead of invisible: the reward-emoji and money-mountain-icon swatch grids (a centred 44px box would overlap adjacent swatches), the 24px ✕ on the photo preview, the 36px recipe favourite heart (already expanded to 44px on the wall profile), and the ✕/emoji toggles inside the three hand-rolled full-screen recipe sheets. **These are still below the house floor and are not fixed by this entry** — they are the next honest a11y pass.
  - **Why a source scan and not a DOM measurement.** jsdom returns `0×0` for every `getBoundingClientRect()`, so any `height < 44` assertion in a unit test passes **vacuously** — the same trap as the earlier "phantom mock" sweep. Contract B already established the `*.tsx`-class-string idiom in this repo for exactly this reason, and the wall profile's 44px floor works the same way (a CSS rule, not a measurement). The suite reads the class attribute of each `<button>`, strips `${…}` holes and conditional `"…"` branches, and flags a sub-44 spacing utility (`h/w-6|7|8|9|10`; **`11` IS 44px** and is the floor).
  - **Tests (TDD, RED first).** NEW `tests/unit/tap-target-contract.test.ts` (3). **RED: 1 failed / 2 passed** — the offender list was exactly `components/tasks/CrewTasksCard.tsx` and `components/leaderboard/DailyQuestCard.tsx`, confirming both the two targets and that the allowlist covered nothing stale. **GREEN: 3/3.** Three guards: a new sub-44 button without `hit-44` fails and must be fixed or given a reason; a **stale** allowlist entry fails (so a fixed or renamed control cannot keep its excuse); and both repaired files are asserted **not** allowlisted, so a future regression surfaces as an offender rather than being excused.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted ESLint 0; `next build` exit 0; full Vitest **4425/4425 (447 files, exit 0)**. Secrets gate `push-safe.sh` CLEAN (exit 0).
  - **Ops:** none — class-name and one stylesheet-contract change; no PB, env, secret or deploy impact. No visual layout change on either control.
  - **Contracts:** the house floor is **44×44** (`AGENTS.md`), and WCAG 2.5.8's 24px is **not** the bar to cite here. A compact control earns its size by carrying `.hit-44`; any new sub-44 `<button>` must carry it or earn a written allowlist entry. The allowlist may only shrink.

- 2026-09-29 — refactor(shell): the side rail is gone; the dock is the whole navigation model — **Files:** `src/components/ui/PageShell.tsx`, DELETED `src/components/ui/SidebarNav.tsx`, DELETED `tests/unit/sidebar-nav.test.tsx`, `src/lib/nav-items.ts`, `src/app/globals.css`, `tests/unit/page-shell-tiers.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/modal-adoption.test.ts`, `docs/UI_AUDIT_2026-09.md`, `AGENTS.md`, this changelog.
  - **The decision (owner's, 2026-09-29).** "We do not want" the left navigation bar. Commit `9d17682` (UI audit Phase 4.1, 2026-09-28) had mounted `SidebarNav` — a fixed `w-60` column of Home/Chat/Meals/Tasks/Rewards/Calendar/HA/Settings — into `PageShell` for parent sessions, so it appeared on **every** route at every width ≥768px instead of only the adult Home screen. It is removed entirely, not re-hidden.
  - **This restores the original design decision, it does not invent a new one.** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-responsive-window-adaptation-design.md:14,65` already ruled the rail out: *"Keep the bottom nav bar on desktop (no side rail / top bar)"* and *"Out of scope: Navigation restructure (no side rail / top bar) — user chose to keep the bottom bar."* Phase 4.1 contradicted a decision the user had already made.
  - **What is removed.** `SidebarNav.tsx` and its 6-test suite are **deleted** (no dead file left behind, matching the Phase 5.1 precedent that deleted the dead stylesheets). `PageShell` drops the `SidebarNav` import and mount, the `useAuth` + `navRoleForUser` calls that existed only to decide the rail, the `md:pl-60` width reservation, the `data-page-rail` attribute, and the wrapper `<div>` that existed only to hold that offset — the content column now sits directly under the shell root. `modal-adoption`'s layer allowlist loses its `SidebarNav` entry, so the `fixed inset-0` layer class is 7 sites, not 8.
  - **Two recorded reasons for 4.1 were factually wrong; corrected in the audit doc and here.** (a) The rail was **not** "rendered only inside `AdultHome`". `src/modes/adult/AdultHome.tsx` has had **zero importers since `0f5bb1e` (2026-08-04)** and is dead code — `src/modes/modes.css:9` says *"FamilyHome/AdultHome remain unwired"* in-tree. For the ~8 weeks before 4.1 the rail therefore rendered on **no route at all**, so 4.1 *added* a left nav everywhere rather than *moving* one. (b) The real defect 4.1 introduced was **two navigations at once**: `CapsuleNav` has no `md:` gate anywhere, so from 768px up a parent saw the rail *and* the floating dock, two independent navs with the same seven destinations. Worse, the dock is centred in the **viewport** (not the offset content column) and sits at `z-50` over the rail's `z-40`, so between **768px and 931px** it painted over and pointer-captured the rail's **Emergency** link — 82px of overlap at iPad-mini portrait, still 49px at iPad Air portrait. iPad portrait is 820px, comfortably over the 768px `md` threshold, so this hit real tablets, not just desktops.
  - **`/emergency` does not become an orphan.** The rail's bottom block was its only inbound link from a nav surface, so its `EXEMPT_ROUTES` reason is corrected to name the routes that actually reach it: **Settings → Safety** (`src/components/settings/SafetySettingsSection.tsx:412`) and the wall Emergency action. `tests/unit/nav-items.test.ts` — the no-orphan contract that walks every `src/app/**/page.tsx` — is green, so this is verified, not asserted.
  - **Contract (TDD, RED first).** `tests/unit/page-shell-tiers.test.tsx` is rewritten from "the rail is present and correctly offset" to the inverse, and the old vacuous half is gone — the reviewer noted the previous suite never asserted `hidden md:flex`, so flipping the rail to always-visible passed all 9. **RED against the unfixed shell: 4 failed / 6 passed** — "renders no left rail for any role" (the parent `nav[aria-label="Main"]` was there), "renders exactly one navigation surface" (2 `<nav>`), "reserves no width for a rail that no longer exists" (`data-page-rail` present), and the content-column test, whose column helper correctly found the rail `<aside>` as the shell's first child. **GREEN: 10/10.** The new pins are behaviour-shaped, not class-string-shaped: no rail **and no `<aside>`** for all four roles (parent/child/pet/guest), exactly one `<nav>`, and no `md:pl-60` anywhere in the rendered HTML. `sidebar-nav.test.tsx`'s 6 tests go with the file.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted ESLint 0 findings; `next build` exit 0; full Vitest **4416/4416 (445 files, exit 0)** — the 4421/4421 (446 files) baseline minus the 6 deleted `sidebar-nav` tests plus this suite's new single-nav pin. **The served bundle was checked, not just the source:** `md:pl-60`, `data-page-rail` and the rail's brand string return **zero** matches across `.next/static/chunks/`, while the dock is still present in 20 chunks.
  - **Ops:** none — no PB collection, field, index, env, secret, migration or `pb:seed` change. Copy and structure only; the rollback twin on the NAS (`consuela-dashboard-old`) stays available.
  - **Contracts:** there is **one** navigation surface — `CapsuleNav`, the bottom dock — on every device, every width and every role; the bottom dock is not a phone-only affordance and must never gain an `md:hidden` sibling. `PageShell` must not re-introduce a fixed-position side column: if a second nav is ever proposed, it replaces the dock rather than sitting beside it. `SidebarNav.tsx` and its suite stay deleted. The manifest in `lib/nav-items.ts` remains the single source of destinations, roles, icons and groups; `PageShell` is not a nav surface and reads nothing from it.

- 2026-09-28 — fix(tasks): streak week scope follows the LOCAL week, not the previous one — **Files:** `src/lib/task-utils.ts`, NEW `tests/unit/streak-week-scope-tz.test.ts`, this changelog.
  - **The bug (pre-existing, not introduced by the points-integrity work).** `calculateRealStreak` and `getThisWeeksCompletedDates` both derived their week key with `mondayOf(new Date(today)).toISOString().split("T")[0]`. `today` is a date-only ISO string, and `new Date("2026-09-28")` parses as **UTC midnight** — which in `America/Detroit` is Sunday 2026-09-27 20:00 **local**. `mondayOf` then normalized that to local midnight (still Sunday) and walked back to **Monday 2026-09-21**. So the whole family's streaks and week-scoped completion lists were computed against the week *before* the one they were living in. The defect only bites on a **Monday** (Tue–Sat land on the same local week, and Sunday lands on the right week by coincidence), which is why the existing pinned suite never saw it: it covers a Wednesday and a Sunday. It is live on the NAS and every family phone, which run `TZ=America/Detroit`. `git log -S` finds no commit in the points-integrity range that introduced it — the same expression is at the merge-base `c3a70fc` and on the audit side `101cddc`. The immediately preceding fix in this series removed the identical defect from `todayMondayISO` and left these two siblings behind.
  - **The fix.** Both call sites now read the canonical `weekStartForDate(today)` from `src/lib/meals-week-utils`, which takes the **date-only string** and does local math from local date parts — one implementation of "string → local week", the idiom already used by `consuela/engine.ts`, `consuela/briefing.ts`, `hermes-tools.ts` and `screensaver/payload.ts`. `localWeekStartISO(new Date(today))` would NOT have worked: `new Date(today)` is already the UTC-midnight instant at fault, and re-formatting it locally yields the *previous* local day. The comment above `mondayOf` — which already claimed "both money-path week keys now read the canonical helper" — is now true, and it also records that `mondayOf` survives only for two test callers: it is `setHours(0,0,0,0)`-based, so it is correct only for an instant already anchored to local midnight and silently wrong for a UTC-parsed one.
  - **Tests (TDD, RED first).** NEW `tests/unit/streak-week-scope-tz.test.ts` (7). RED against the unfixed code: **4 failed / 3 passed** — Detroit Monday returned last week's Friday in the week list (`expected [ '2026-09-25T10:00:00.000Z', …(1) ] to deeply equal [ '2026-09-28T10:00:00.000Z' ]`) and a Monday with an empty current week inherited five prior-week completions; the two `TZ=Asia/Tokyo` cases failed on the mirror defect (the week key serialized a day early, so a Monday counted the preceding Sunday and a Sunday counted the Sunday before it — `expected [ '2026-09-21', …(6) ] to deeply equal [ '2026-09-21', …(5) ]`). GREEN: 7/7. The Sunday and mid-week cases pass in both states by construction (they are regression guards, not evidence of the bug) and are kept so the whole week arithmetic is pinned, not just the Monday edge. The Tokyo block is **meaningful, not inert**: `vitest.config.ts` pins `test.env.TZ = "America/Detroit"`, and the block re-pins `process.env.TZ` per test — that is why both Tokyo cases are RED against the old code, and `afterEach` restores Detroit so no other file inherits it.
  - **Findings, deliberately NOT fixed here (out of scope for this defect, listed for triage).** A sweep of `src/` for the same defect class found: `src/app/tasks/page.tsx:84-86,135-136` (`isoOffset` / `getDueOptions` serialize a local-shifted instant with `toISOString().split("T")[0]`, so "Tomorrow", the weekday quick-adds and the 31-day due list are a day ahead every evening 20:00–24:00 local); `src/modes/kid/KidHome.tsx:401` ("completed today" / "points today" compares against `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)`, so after 20:00 local a kid's own evening completions vanish from the today card); `src/modes/adult/AdultHome.tsx:154` (`days[new Date(d).getDay()]` on open-meteo's date-only `daily.time` → every forecast weekday label shows the previous local day); `src/lib/schedule-analytics.ts:340,357` (`new Date(t.due)` on a date-only due date → an overdue count that includes tasks due *today*); `src/lib/skill-tree.ts:201` (`new Date(lastActivityDate).toDateString()` against a UTC instant, same evening-only skew); and a set of UTC-day-as-calendar-day keys where the skew is cosmetic (chat thread ids, Google quota windows, the Analytics 30-day default).
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full Vitest **4421/4421 (446 files, exit 0)** — the 445-file / 4414-test baseline plus this suite's 7.
  - **Ops:** none — no PB collection, field, index, env, secret, or deploy change; the fix is client/display-side week math plus its test.
  - **Contracts:** a date-only ISO string is a **local** calendar date and must never round-trip through `new Date(string)` before date or week math; the week key for "the week containing this date" is `weekStartForDate(string)`, and the week key for "this week" is `localWeekStartISO()` — nothing else, on the money path or the leaderboard path; `mondayOf(Date)` is not a week-key helper and is never handed a UTC-parsed instant.

- 2026-09-28 — refactor(tasks): split the 2,946-line Tasks page by section (UI audit Phase 5.7) — **Files:** NEW `src/components/tasks/{TasksStats,CrewTasksCard,TasksArchive,TasksRewardsPanel}.tsx`, `src/app/tasks/page.tsx`, NEW `tests/unit/tasks-sections.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/tablet-two-column.test.ts`, `docs/UI_AUDIT_2026-09.md`, `AGENTS.md`, this changelog.
  - **What the audit found (finding 20, P2):** `src/app/tasks/page.tsx` was 2,943 lines with 33 `useState` — the family's daily driver and the hardest file in the repo to change safely.
  - **The split:** four self-contained view blocks moved to `src/components/tasks/` behind typed, documented props — `TasksStats` (the 3-up stat row + the Tasks/Leaderboard switch), `CrewTasksCard` (parent-only crew management, now reading the shared `task-utils` crew helpers directly), `TasksArchive` (the deep archive: TreasurePath + AchievementWall + FamilyGoal + HallOfFame + the recent-activity feed) and `TasksRewardsPanel` (rewards and penalties cards, incl. the AI-suggestion strip). All 33 `useState`, the PB sync flow and the PIN/edit modals **stay in the page** — the modals own page state, so moving them would have been prop-drilling state, not a split. 2,946 → 2,820 lines with zero behavior change.
  - **Left for follow-up (honestly):** the tasks tab's own body — the add/edit form plus the four task boards (Open, Pending, Needs approval, Completed) — is the remaining tangle. It is one entangled block (a 40-prop extraction would be a reshuffle, not a split), so it stays until its state can move into a `useTaskBoards` hook in a change of its own.
  - **Contract:** NEW `tests/unit/tasks-sections.test.tsx` (4) — each section renders from props and fires its handlers, the crew gate hides when the viewer is not a parent, and the page provably no longer carries the moved markup while still owning the data flow. The Phase 4.3 tablet contract now reads `md:col-span-2` from `TasksStats` (the stat row moved) — a contract that follows the code, not a snapshot of it.
  - **Verified:** full Vitest **3,373/3,373 (403 files, exit 0)** including all 17 `tasks-*` suites (78 tests) unchanged, `tsc --noEmit` clean, targeted ESLint 0 findings, `next build` exit 0.

- 2026-09-28 — feat(a11y): every hand-rolled overlay earns the dialog contract, and the ratio is pinned (UI audit Phase 5.4) — **Files:** `src/components/ui/MemberModal.tsx`, `src/components/auth/PinModal.tsx`, `src/components/leaderboard/LevelUpModal.tsx`, `src/components/skill-tree/QuestDetail.tsx`, `src/components/money-mountain/{TransactionLogger,CreateMountainForm}.tsx`, `src/components/ha/AlarmPinModal.tsx`, `src/components/meals/{StorePicker,RecipeModal,RecipeSearchModal,RecipeImportModal,CookMode}.tsx`, `src/components/profile/PhotoCropEditor.tsx`, `src/components/time-capsule/{ContentUploader,CreateCapsuleForm}.tsx`, NEW `tests/unit/modal-adoption.test.ts`, `docs/UI_AUDIT_2026-09.md`, `AGENTS.md`, this changelog.
  - **What the audit found (finding 11, P2):** 24 hand-rolled `fixed inset-0` overlays against a single `Modal` that owns the focus trap, Escape and scroll lock — 1 : 24 — with no role, no `aria-modal` and (in `RecipeModal`) no Escape handler.
  - **The fix, in two passes:** (1) every overlay that is genuinely a dialog and must stay custom — full-screen recipe/search/import sheets, the photo editor, cook mode, weather details, the wall PIN pad, the PIN/level/quest/transaction/mountain/alarm dialogs, store & capsule forms, member editor — now carries `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"` and a human `aria-label`; `RecipeModal` also closes on Escape like its siblings. (2) the eight `fixed inset-0` sites that are *not* dialogs (confetti, celebrations, the shell root, the dock, the rail, the screensaver) are classified as layers, so the ratio means something.
  - **The contract:** NEW `tests/unit/modal-adoption.test.ts` (4) allowlists every hand-rolled overlay **with a written reason** (why it is not ported yet), fails on a NEW hand-rolled overlay, requires the dialog semantics on the dialog class, pins the hand-rolled-dialog count as a ceiling that only shrinks as files port to `Modal`, and asserts `Modal.tsx` still owns `role="dialog"` + Escape + scroll handling. Each port to `Modal` lowers the ceiling in the same commit that removes the file from the list.
  - **Caveat:** the centered dialogs (`MemberModal`, `PinModal`, `TransactionLogger`, …) are semantics-complete but still custom; porting them to `Modal` is mechanical follow-up work the ceiling now tracks. The dead `kitchen-text` class also left `CookMode`.
  - **Verified:** full Vitest **3,369/3,369 (402 files, exit 0)**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, targeted ESLint 0 errors (7 pre-existing warnings), `next build` exit 0.

- 2026-09-28 — feat(design-system): one page that audits itself (UI audit Phase 5.6) — **Files:** DELETED `src/app/_design-system/page.tsx`, NEW `src/components/design-system/DesignSystemSelfAudit.tsx`, `src/app/design-system/page.tsx`, `src/lib/nav-items.ts`, NEW `tests/unit/design-system-self-audit.test.tsx`, `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`, `docs/UI_AUDIT_2026-09.md`, `AGENTS.md`, this changelog.
  - **What the audit found (findings 17–19 + doc drift):** `/_design-system` and `/design-system` were near-duplicate previews, each with its own production gate, and the doc's story about the rewrite was wrong (it lives in `src/middleware.ts`, not `next.config.ts`).
  - **One page:** the `_` duplicate is deleted. The legacy URL keeps working — middleware still rewrites `/_design-system` → `/design-system` (unchanged, and `middleware-gate.test.ts` keeps asserting it) — and the now-stale `EXEMPT_ROUTES` entry is removed so the no-orphan contract stays honest.
  - **The live audit:** the surviving page ends with `DesignSystemSelfAudit`, which runs the four house rules against its own mounted DOM in the browser — sub-12px text, tap targets under 44×44 that lack `hit-44` (unrendered elements are skipped; `hit-44`'s `::before` expansion is honoured), interactive elements with no accessible name (aria-label / aria-labelledby / title / own text / wrapped label), and resting inset box-shadows. Opt-outs: `data-ds-shadow-exempt` (the glossy demo chrome) and `data-ds-audit-ignore` (the audit card itself). Counts render per category with the first three offenders and a Re-scan button; `scanDesignSystem(root)` is exported as a pure function so tests run it over a synthetic root — no axe dependency in the app bundle (axe-core remains the e2e guardrail).

Migrating every one is a file-by-file effort, so the contract pins them as a **budget** (same idea as the ambient-motion budget): the counts may fall as files adopt `Surface`/`--neu-*`, but a new literal fails CI — the metric can only improve.

Card/kitchen text now reads the same AA tokens as the rest of the app.

Expanding is session-only — a reload folds again. The wall and desktop render everything (columns + height for it).

Outside a provider the hook returns `true`, so every other route is unchanged. Ranking + folding compound with it: folded widgets never mount, so their loops never run.

WALL_GRID_CLASS : …` in `src/app/page.tsx`) — now pinned by contract; the data screens get their wall composition from 4.3's md two-column idiom (the wall's 1080px portrait viewport sits above the md tier), so no screen needed a bespoke wall layout.

- 2026-09-28 — feat(responsive): tablet two-column on Home's grid idiom (UI audit Phase 4.3) — **Files:** `src/app/tasks/page.tsx`, `src/app/calendar/page.tsx`, `src/components/meals/PlanTab.tsx`, `src/components/meals/ShopTab.tsx`, `src/components/meals/StockTab.tsx`, `src/components/meals/RecipeBox.tsx`, NEW `tests/unit/tablet-two-column.test.ts`, `docs/UI_AUDIT_2026-09.md`, `AGENTS.md`, this changelog.
  - **What the audit found (finding 7):** at 1440×900 `/tasks` still rendered one narrow column (a private `lg:max-w-3xl` inside the shell), and each meals surface had invented its own late breakpoint and column template — `lg:grid-cols-[1fr_320px]` (Plan/Stock), `xl:grid-cols-[1fr_280px]` (Shop), `xl:grid-cols-3` (RecipeBox), plus `xl:order-*` / `hidden xl:*` / `xl:hidden` hooks — so no two screens tiered the same way, and none of them used Home's idiom.
  - **The convergence:** every split is now `grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-6` with `col-span-*`/`order-*` at `md:` — the same vocabulary as `HOME_GRID_FALLBACK`. **Tasks** drops `lg:max-w-3xl` (PageShell owns width tiers) and splits: stats span both columns (`md:col-span-2`), the view switch sits left, the task board / leaderboard panel right. **Calendar** pairs month grid | selected-day agenda — the agenda is pinned `md:col-start-2 md:row-start-1` so the parent week card flows `md:col-span-2` beneath them and the form/upcoming cards auto-flow after; the schedule tab stays single-column (routines are one grouped list — two columns would strand half-width groups). **Meals** Plan/Shop/Stock pair main content with their summary rail at `md:`, ShopTab's desktop-bulk-bar/mobile-sticky-bar pair moves `xl:` → `md:`, and the rail order resolves to `order-first md:order-none`; **RecipeBox** drops `xl:grid-cols-3` (the `sm:grid-cols-2` tier carries). **Settings** already tiers at/below the tablet bucket (`grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2` on the launcher; section pages are single-purpose panels) — covered by contract, not churn.
  - **Contract:** NEW `tests/unit/tablet-two-column.test.ts` (5) fails if a meals tab reintroduces `lg:`/`xl:` column templates or `xl:` hooks, if Tasks regains a private max-width, if Calendar loses the agenda placement / week-card span, or if the Settings launcher loses its sub-md two-column tier.
